Actually the books sell themselves. Since they are incarnations of Krsna, we just act as Krsna’s carriers. If they also receive tasty prasad they will never lament having spent so much money on these books. It is very interesting that Jumbalat, the famous Druze leader, knew Sanskrit, was vegetarian, and translated a book about Krsna. The fact that Mr. Falah is trying to order fifty sets for all his institutions reminds me of the glorious Library Party of the 1970s. Srila Prabhupada started it with Satsvarapa dasa Goswami and called it the most important work. Keep it up.

In one sense, we should not be so surprised by the recent rising results in Israel. Krsna is Ananta, unlimited, and He can spread His glories everywhere in a moment. When Krsna sees His devotees are eager to assist Him, He arranges everything so that mysteriously the devotees are triumphant. Look how the Pandavas were victorious in eighteen days against all material odds.

January 20, 1989—Saguna dasi to Danavir dasa:

Book distribution is very auspicious and Israel *yatra* is like a bright sun. Among the Druze we have a chanter. Isvara Krsna and Dhira Govinda Prabhus gave him a set and beads. The Druze have their own *japa* of ninety-nine beads on which they chant ninety-nine qualities of the Lord, such as great and merciful. As Kali-yuga was progressing, they accepted a sixty-six-bead *japa,* then thirty-three beads. Some of the younger generation is affected by Israelism, and they reject reincarnation and become great materialists. I feel they need book distribution urgently before they mess everything up....Second Canto is very beautiful (*karmis* say), and now Third Canto is being paged in Italy.

January 22, 1989—Danavir dasa (Los Angeles) to Santasya (Haifa):

The miraculous stories of the Druze people buying sets of books continues to amaze everyone who hears them. The story included in your letter about the morning you and Saguna dasi sold six sets is wonderful. You are all tasting the excitement of book distribution as the earlier devotees did while Srila Prabhupada was present. Since we obviously have no potency of our own, and all of our strength is reflected from Srila Prabhpupada’s glowing desires and devotion, it must be concluded that he, Srila Prabhupada, is still present with us. This is the correct conclusion. “He lives forever and his follower lives with him.” You all are living with Srila Prabhupada because you have taken his life’s work as your life. All glories to your service!

February 9, 1989—Jayadvaita Swami (Vrndavana, India) to DG (Haifa):

The news of the preaching among the Druze is wonderful to hear. So go on distributing books with full enthusiasm. Now we have the Second Canto too, and it seems to have come out very nicely. We can go on printing and distributing like this canto after canto.

When you meet intelligent young men who might be fit to translate our books, it’s good to get their names and addresses so that we can get in touch with them again.

Israel is a wonderful field, and ours are wonderful books. So preach and be happy.

Danavir dasa (San Diego) to DG (Haifa):

I have received all three ecstatic letters dated Dec. 5, 10, and 13, 1988. These continuing *sankirtana* stories are truly phenomenal. Over thirty sets in a week; twelve sets in a day; six sets in two hours. It’s a special reward for our Israeli *yatra* for having perservered with enthusiasm, determination, and patience over many years. It encourages me greatly to perform my humble duty here. Please continue with increasing desire and Krsna will open more and more doors and hearts.

Regarding Arabic books in Cairo: the Arabic books were not in Cairo but in Beirut, and were not *Bhagavad-gitas.* I know of a large quantity of smaller books which were stored by the printer in Beirut. The devotees left the country and could not pay for the books. There were bombs exploding everywhere. Anyway, according to Tribhuvanatha Prabhu, who is now in the U.K. and who ordered the books in Beirut, there is no feasible way devotees could approach those books, if they still exist, because the printer might become angry. Arabic books are available and one can order them either from Harivilasa Prabhu in Berkeley or from Bhakti Ananda-svarupa Maharaja in Amsterdam.

March 19, 1989—Danavir dasa (Los Angeles) to ML:

...Dhira Govinda Prabhu sent me some nice photos of the meeting with Mr. Falah as well as Salach Habas, other fortunate Druze, and finally the SKP team with books in hands. I think the whole story should be compiled as an article for the BTG.

When Uukadeva Gosvami traveled across the countryside, it increased his realization of the Lord’s glories. Similarly, as all of you are journeying across deserts, to the seasides, to the pastures, and even to the Israeli Himalayas, the transcendental realizations are expanding by Lord Caitanya’s grace.

March 24, 1989—ML (Tel Aviv) to Danavir dasa:

It is two days after Gaura-Purnima. We had a very nice festival and feast and the devotees arranged three plays about the early pastimes of Lord Gauranga. I am enclosing a few pictures.

Somehow or other we have developed a very friendly relationship with all the neighbors and shop owners in the area \[Tel Aviv\]. Behind every door in the building is a smiling face of a neighbor who has gotten many plates of *prasadam* and has heard *mangala-arati* with no fail for the last year. Next-door neighbor asked me to dress her daughter like a devotee for Purim celebration, and even Dhira Govinda Prabhu thought that she was a devotee visiting from abroad. The shopkeepers are greeting us nicely with, “Hare Krsna,” and sometimes give donations. One neighbor asked me the meaning of the prayers we chant and another brought us flowers for our birthday. Next-door neighbor is an old lady dying from cancer, and she is very fortunate to hear *kirtanas* and taste *prasadam* and get garlands of flowers.

*Sankirtana*is going on nicely. santasya, Saguna, and Nagapatni Prabhus are staying in Haifa, and Dolayatra, myself, and the men are in Tel Aviv.

Santasya has had very nice success in giving sets to Muslim lawyers in their offices in Haifa and Tel Aviv. Then they went to Muslim villages in the north. We also went to some Muslim villages in the area of Tel Aviv. I think I can realize more what Srila Prabhupada meant when he said that he takes the dust of the feet of devotees. They are different from Christian and Druze Arabs about the Koran.

People are talking about an independent Palestinian state in the West Bank. They think that they can go on living sinful life slaughtering cows as well as their own children and not suffer some reaction.

So my prayers to Lord Nrsimhadeva are increasing every day. I feel great relief when everyone is back from *sankirtana* safe and sound.

March 26, 1989—DG to Danavir dasa:

Since Isvara Krsna Prabhu left for Italy–India, Parama-guru and I have been based in Tel Aviv with Maha-Laksmi and at least one other *mataji* living in the roof apartment. For the first weeks, Santasya and Saguna were in Haifa with the Suzuki (Saguna is a driver), and in recent weeks Nagapatni has joined their party. Either way, the results have been nice. In Tel Aviv I’ve gotten back into the contact program I was doing two years ago—making appointments with New Agers, book collectors, etc. Results have been encouraging. One story: In 1987 a religious Jewish lawyer took a Hebrew set from me. I revisited him and he took forty-eight books—thirty English *Bhagavatams,* seventeen English Cc., and Second Canto in Hebrew.

The Druze not only take books, but they’re enthusiastic about *kirtana, prasad,* and philosophical discussion also.... The week before Gaura-Purnima, the devotees did about 390 book points. The takers were not Druze, or even Christians, but a few Jews and mostly Muslims. We’ve entered into the field of Muslim villages (not in occupied territory). The experience has been that the intellectuals are very enthusiastic to take sets, or at least books, but we should stay away from preaching on the street where there is risk of agitating some people. So we go directly to the schools, lawyers, etc., parking the car in private areas. Practically every library has taken. We will be careful. Generally the political situation in Israel is worsening. The more I talk to all different peoples here, the more I’m convinced that Krsna consciousness, and specifically book distribution, is the only solution. Each side is angry and intolerant

Right near Frishman there was recently an incident The neighbors in this building are our very good friends. Next door, the lady is about to die of cancer. She accepts the devotees as family, and enjoys hearing the *mangala-arati* each morning. She has pictures from BTG to look at, and we gave her the garland of Lord Caitanya from Gaura-Purnima

April 18, 1989—DG to Danavir dasa:

We also have a good *bhakta* prospect amongst the Muslims. We’re fully aware that we need to be very careful in cultivating him Also, last week Isvara Krsna, Maha-Laksmi, and I did a program in a Druze village. Six young Druze attended, three of whom already had sets. It could develop into a *nama-hatta* group

April 18, 1989—DG to various devotees involved with Arabic preaching:

I’m writing to inform you of progress made in the field of preaching to Muslims, as I’ve heard you have some direct interest in this area. Probably you’ve heard something about the Druze preaching from devotees in India (Isvara Krsna dasa from Israel) or IWR. Recently, the *sankirtana* devotees have been going to Muslim villages with very nice success. The intellectuals are very eager to take Srila Prabhupada’s books. More than thirty villages have taken sets for their libraries and schools. This past week we held a program at a small Muslim village called Kabul. The program was attended by about fifty intellectuals from the Muslim community, who all appreciated the philosophy, *prasad,* and Vaisnava culture. Also*, nama-hatta groups* and *bhakta* prospects are arising from the book distribution.

P. S. We’ve met many Israeli Arabs who say they could help us sell books in the Arabic countries if we had books in Arabic.

April 22, 1989—Danavir dasa (San Diego) to ML:

It is nice how you preached to the Palestinian who said he couldn’t take up KC until they were free from foreign subjugation. Krsna consciousness is so important that it cannot wait. Even when the language is different, you are communicating to them the urgency, just like when a house is on fire, somehow the neighbors get the message across despite a foreign dialect. This type of bold preaching can actually bring the peaceful solution to all the Middle East problems.

April 26, 1989—Mr. Jamil Mahajna (Uhm El Fahm) to Maha-Laksmi dasi:

For Mrs. Maha-Laksmi

My dear Maha,

I thank you for your note and for your warm words. Please allow me to express my admiration for you. I was very impressed from your culture and open-mindedness and mental sharpness when we had an enjoyable and interesting conversation in the house. Your friend was mostly most of the time remembering the names of Krsna.

Please let me speak to your common sense because “Thy shall love thy’s friend as thyself.” There is absolutely no such thing as reincarnation. We continue in our graves till the day of judgement, when our Divine Lord, may He be blessed, revives us for giving us the reward or punishment for our deeds in the world of today. Reincarnation is a complete contradiction to the day of judgment.

A second point according to the Indian philosophy of reincarnation: the soul passes through the body of a dog and the body of a snake and the body of an ass or a horse and then passes to the body of a human being. There is no such thing because we are the descendants of Adam and Eve and we have inherited all the biological and emotional characteristics of these two parents. It is impossible for a human to give birth to dog or for an ass to give birth to a snake or a snake to give birth to a lion. These things do not make sense. Nevertheless, if there is such a thing as reincarnation so at least a human’s soul should to a human body and a snake soul to a snake body. I do not know that there is reincarnation to animals as well!!

According to the doctrine of reincarnation the souls of the Brahmins do not reincarnate, and those good people as well as other good people, their souls do not reincarnate. It is very mystifying reincarnation simultaneously exists and doesn’t exist! Maha, my friend, let us put ourselves in place of those Brahmins for a second. We are mortal and are all haters of death by the nature of our existence, and all of us, if given the opportunity, do not want death and want to continue to live forever and ever. Back to the reincarnation. Brahmins as well as the simple people want to come back to life after death many times. Be honest with yourself. You do not want to come back to life again and again!!

My dear Maha, I am not aspiring nor am I inclined that you will change your religion. It is true that the Indian philosophy has enamored you. I am all hope that you will take interest in the Islam—the philosophy and the civilization of the Islam. I can help you in this matter in many ways if you are interested. Remain a Buddhist, but read about the Islam.

Best wishes for Noga our friend and Shalom!

P. S. I can meet with Maha again.

With due respects,

Mr. Jamil Mahajna

May 25, 1989—Trai das (Villagio Hare Krsna, Italy) to DG:

Sometime ago I received a copy of a letter about the book distribution explosion there in Israel that you wrote to Danavir Prabhu. It was very ecstatic to read, and I am sure that if you stick tightly to just books Prabhupada will shower his blessings on the *yatra*. One thing is that I am very interested to help with translating books into Arabic and distributing them. There are some books, *Coming Back—Reincarnation* in Arabic in Paris. If you want some, let me know with all details how to send them. The other very important thing is that you mention one man in your letter who is Arabic speaking, vegetarian and chants rounds. If you could please give me his address and name I could contact him about doing some translating work. Thank you very much for this help.

May 25, 1989—Dolayatra dasi to Danavir dasa:

Dhira Govinda and Maha-Laksmi Prabhus are going to U.S. today, so I’m sending a small letter with them.

Here we continue to distribute Srila Prabhupada’s books mainly to Arab people because they take them very easily. We mainly approach the intellectuals and they take the books (sets) without asking too many questions about them and without arguing (like the Jews tend to do). I hope that they’ll read the books as well because they can get a lot of knowledge out of them.

P. S. Just few moments ago a lady who took a *Bhagavad-gita* from me a few months ago came here. She said that she has read the *Bhagavad-gita* from the beginning to the end and that she would like to buy the other five books. And she did. All glories to Srila Prabhupada!

May 26, 1989—Danavir dasa (San Diego) to PG (Tel Aviv):

Yes, I have heard about some of the fantastic successes in Muslim preaching work going on in Israel. And the most educated leaders within their communities are buying our books by the set. It is not an ordinary thing that Muslims are buying these books from their, “materially speaking,” archenemies. It indicates that the *sankirtana* movement is being conducted on the spiritual platform. *Sarvo-padhi-vinirmuktam, tat-paratvena nirmalam/ hrsikena hrsikesa-sevanam bhaktir ucyate. Bhakti,* or devotional service, means engaging all our senses in the service of the Lord, the Supreme Personality of Godhead, the master of all the senses.

June 21, 1989—Danavir dasa (San Diego) to DG (Philadelphia):

The Muslim preaching is wonderful and amazing. I think that since it is favorable, why not continue to develop that field. Lord Caitanya Mahaprabhu left Bengal for riper preaching fields, and Srila Prabhupada strategically exited India for the eager American audiences. Lord Krsna’s devotional service is full of adventure and surprises. *Param vijayate sri Krsna sankirtanam.*

Please give my warm regards to all the devotees serving Lord Caitanya’s glorious mission in Israel when you return.

June 4, 1989—Danavir dasa (Potomac, Maryland) to ML (Philadelphia):

Your letter describing the Muslim high school manager who took a set is phenomenal. It definitely proves that Lord Caitanya is present with the *sankirtana* devotees. It should be sent in to the WSN as soon as possible.

July 13, 1989—Harikesa Swami to DG:

I am sorry to say that I have not had any Arabic books in stock since perhaps ten years. There are absolutely none of them left, so there is no question of giving any to you for preaching in Israel. I am sure that you would have a good success with such books if you had them. Perhaps you should organize the translation of some small books in Arabic, and then you would be able to print them and distribute them there as you like?

August 16, 1989—Bhakti-caru Swami to DG (Osafia):

Thank you very much for your concern regarding printing and distribution of Srila Prabhupada’s books in Israel. I am going to write Harivilasa Prabhu and ask him how he can help you. Is he the BBT trustee for Arabic books? He has always been a very enthusiastic publisher of Srila Prabhupada’s books. Therefore I am sure he will try to do whatever he can. You can also write to Jayadvaita Maharaja, because he is the BBT trustee for Israel. I will personally speak with him when I meet him next. I will try to help you in whichever way I can. But to tell you frankly, my involvement in BBT is very, very little .

Autumn 1989—PG to Danavir dasa:

An electrician fixed the TV for a set of Russian and Hebrew *Gitas.* An Arab worker at a gas station filled the tank for the value of an Arabic *Gita*. And the biggest miracle was Akko Theatre Festival. I was participating with a book stand for five days.

September 1989—Santasya to Danavir dasa:

Also, we got the blessings from all concerned in the matter of printing Arabic *Gitas*. It will be done soon probably, in a few weeks or so. Actually it can be extremely nice because it will increase book distribution tremendously. Also in that way more people can understand the philosophy and not just buy the books out of piety, which may result in Arab devotees, Arabs preaching in their own countries, etc. I met this week a Druze teacher who took a set some months ago, when the “set explosion” was at its beginning, and now he is so learned in the books. He asked me if in Israel we also have a temple like those Srila Prabhupada built in India. I apologized and said not exactly like that. Then he said, “The book *Prabhupada* said that you can have a temple everywhere, you just need to have ” Then I jumped and said, “A picture of Lord Krsna.” He said, “No, the *murti*.” And on and on he mentioned many points from the *Gita* and *Bhagavatam* enough enlivening to give these people more of Prabhupada’s mercy.

*Sankirtana*is very nice. Arab villages are filled with Srila Prabhupada’s books now, and their future is more and more advancement in *Krsna-prema.*

September 11, 1989—Maha-Laksmi dasi (Haifa) to Jadurani dasi (NY):

I think that your program of encouraging “old women” and training *bhaktins* on *sankirtana* is very wonderful. I read about it in the WSN. Soon we will get *Bhagavad-gitas* in Arabic, and I think this will fuel our book distribution and *laksmi* collection very much. Despite the fact that the political situation in Israel is very, very tense. It’s like a volcanic mountain about to explode.

A few days ago the *matajis* went to a Muslim village and showed the books to a man that proudly said that his uncle is a terrorist that killed sixty Israelies and that they worship him just like God.... They started to shake like leaves in autumn. It’s amazing how we are freely moving among these Palestinian Arabs who like very much to kill Israelies and somehow or other some of them transcend the bodily conception of life and take interest in the profound wisdom of *Bhagavad-gita* and *Srimad-Bhagavatam.* It is a great adventure. We go to villages that no Israeli dares to step there and directly to the sheikh of the village who welcomes us very nicely. By Krsna’s mercy they have a very nice principle, which is very similar to the Vedic culture, that if a guest comes to their house they give him all hospitality, even if he is their greatest enemy. So we present the books to them and they mostly take. Santasya Prabhu has even met a man who was just released from prison after staying there for five years. He was accused of being a political threat to the country, having Palestinian point of view. This man has spent five years just reading books of philosophy, and he has even read the *Bhagavad-gita*. He told her that the Israeli jailkeepers would torture him very severely, binding him in the jail courtyard in a way that he had to stand up for ten days, and constantly whipping, torturing, and humiliating him. Being humiliated for an Arab man is worse than death, and he could burn to ashes the three worlds just out of revenge, but still, seeing her innocence and purity, he gave her $100 for a set of six books and promised to read them.

On another occasion Santasya Prabhu and I met an Arab person who accompanied us in the village. He participated in all the mantras, or practically speaking forced the villagers (who are all his friends) to take the books and if they didn’t he would pay for their book. Later, since we had no transportation back to the temple on that day, he forced his cousin to drive us all the way back to the temple (about a fifty-minute drive). This man is the only strict vegetarian Arab in his village.... We invited him into the temple and gave him some *prasadam* and taught him to chant *japa.* It is unheard of for Arab people that a woman walks around in the village to sell books to a man, what to speak of an Israeli woman .. . what to speak of an Israeli woman dressed in an Indian sari with a red dot on her forehead. This blows their minds completely. We are the only so-called Israelies placing our feet in these Arab villages because they are scared to death from these people, what to speak of inviting them to their houses, what to speak of letting them drive you home in their car at the end of the day in the small and undeveloped roads of the village. But Krsna is the Supreme Controller, and *sankirtana* is *ahaituki,* and by the mercy of Lord Caitanya all of this is possible.

Like a few weeks ago we met a Druze Arab that told us that in his previous life he remembers being a yogi in the Himalayas and that he feels love for Krsna very deep inside him. He also purchased a set of books for $100, even though he could barely read Hebrew.

October 22, 1989—fax from DG (Haifa) and Bhakta Salach (Rama) to Kirtiraja dasa (Sweden):

Dear Kirtiraja Prabhu,

The following is a letter from Bhakta Salach, translated from the original Hebrew:

Dear Kirtiraja, I am very happy that you visited Israel and the village of Rama with the Hare Krsna devotees. One of my great hopes was that the *Bhagavad-gita* will be spread to the Arab speaking citizens. Now, by Krsna’s mercy, this is happening. My request is that you will help us to attain a higher understanding of *bhakti-yoga* and that, by your mercy, we will be able to do a little bit to help others. Thank you, Salach.

Bhakta Salach has been chanting a minimum of sixteen rounds per day, as well as offering his food and following the principles. He’s already sold fifteen books in his village and has orders for more. He has initiated the project of obtaining permission from the local council of Rama that we will be able to perform *kirtana* in the village. Also, he’s translated and is helping us to print flyers in Arabic. He’s very enthusiastic and is eager to advance. Last week, Maha-Laksmi and I did an evening program in Rama. It was a great success. Many elderly gentlemen attended, including your Rajasthani farmer. We distributed prasad and held *kirtana,* lecture, and discussion. It’s really an ecstatic *nama-hatta* village. After the program we had a *japa* session, with many members of the community participating. The book distribution is going nicely. The first two weeks we’ve given more than seventy Maha big books, including a thirty-volume *Bhagavatam* set in English to a Druze high school principal. I think if you respond to Salach, he’ll be really enlivened.

October 28, 1989—ML to Danavir dasa:

We are so busy now, we have a big mission to distribute all the Arabic *Gitas* which were recently printed. Dhira Govinda Prabhu and I are based in the Haifa apt. and are concentrating 100% on selling the Arabic books.

We have some really nice contacts and devotee prospects. Salach Abas from the village Rama is chanting sixteen rounds, following the regulative principles, and distributing Arabic *Gitas* to whomever he meets. The system is that we leave twelve books with him and he sells them and gives us the money, or more precisely, gives Krsna the money. He has also translated the flyer, “Purity is the Force” into Arabic and is helping us to print it.

Two days ago we met Mr. Samich Natoor in the Druze village Daliyat El Carmel, and maybe he will be the person that will help us to break into the walls of the many Arabic countries surrounding Israel. He is a seller of Arabic books. He has contacts with all the Arabic countries around Israel as well as with all Arab villages in Israel. He purchased ten books from us at a low price to start selling them to schools and libraries. He also suggested to join us and to open a center in Egypt, but when he heard about the four regulative principles, he was somewhat reluctant. Anyway, he can be a great tool in the hand of Lord Caitanya to spread Krsna consciousness. He is going to send one copy of *Bhagavad-gita* to a Druze friend of his in Cyprus who is exporting books to Lebanon, so we shall see what is Krsna’s plan in this connection.

P. S. There is a very nice story which I would like to tell you. A few days ago we met a Druze that told us that in his previous life he remembers being a yogi in the Himalayas and that he feels love for Krsna deep inside him. He said, “As much as you love Krsna, I love him deep inside me more than you.”

October 26, 1989—DG and ML (Haifa) to Samich Natoor (Daliyat El Carmel) (Translated from Hebrew):

Dear Samich,

Hare Krsna. Please accept our greetings. We would like to thank you for hosting us in your house this past Tuesday afternoon. It was a real pleasure to talk with you.

We are enclosing a copy of a page from a book written by Kamal Jumbalat in Arabic, where he writes about *Bhagavad-gita,* Krsna and Arjuna. Also, we are including an excerpt from Jumbalat’s book, *I Speak for Lebanon,* where he praises Krsna. The *Bhagavad-gita* was spoken by Krsna to Arjuna 5,000 years ago, before the famous battle of Kuruksetra. There are many other writings where Jumbalat glorifies the *Bhagavad-gita* and other literature from ancient India. We are confident that Druze in other countries will greatly appreciate the *Bhagavad-gita As It Is* in the Arabic language.

Within Israel, the Druze Ministry of Education has purchased 300 books (fifty, six-book sets) in Hebrew from the Hare Krsna movement for their educational institutions. In addition, the religious, cultural, intellectual, and political leaders of the Druze in Israel have purchased thousands of our books over the past year, in recognition of their authentic spiritual wisdom. We have done public programs in the villages of Daliyat, Maghar, and Rama, and have had a very favorable meeting with Sheikh Tarif Amin (pictures are available if you want). The other enclosures are of international publications in English that our movement publishes. Several articles have been printed about how the Druze are taking a great interest in the Vedic literatures and in Krsna consciousness. There’s much more material of this sort if you would like copies.

For the Muslim countries, you can emphasize the introduction to the Arabic *Gita* written by the famous Muslim sheikh.

Generally, it should be mentioned that the *Bhagavad-gita* is the most important literature from the East. It was spoken 5,000 years ago. It is the authoritative book about all yoga systems and ancient Indian culture and philosophy. All the great thinkers throughout history from Socrates and Aristotle to Albert Einstein and Kamal Jumbalat have studied *Bhagavad-gita*. This edition, *Bhagavad-gita As It Is,* translated and explained by His Divine Grace Srila Prabhupada, is particularly authoritative because of Srila Prabhupada’s presenting exactly the meaning of Krsna. This edition of *Bhagavad-gita* is used in universities all over the Western world and in India. Srila Prabhupada is not only the topmost Vedic scholar of the modern age, but is also a pure devotee of Sri Krsna, the original speaker of *Bhagavad-gita*. This *Bhagavad-gita* is also the best-selling edition. We are sure there will be a nice market for this book in Arabic countries if it is presented properly. Please read the *Bhagavad-gita* and the other books in the series. We’re looking forward to further contact with you. Hoping this finds you and your family in the best of health. Hare Krsna.

October 29, 1989—DG to Danavir dasa:

There’s some real exciting developments on the preaching scene here. Maha-Laksmi and I are based in Haifa. We have a spiritual (*sadhana*) program in the morning and go to Arab villages during the day. The response to the Arabic *Gita* is very nice. One Druze boy, Bhakta Salach, from Rama village, is chanting at least sixteen rounds per day, following the principles, and offering all his food with Srila Prabhupada’s *pranama-mantras.* In addition, he’s selling many books in his village, is helping us translate and print Arabic flyers, and is working to obtain official permission from the Rama City Council to allow us to do *kirtana* in the village. Last week, Maha-Laksmi and I did a program in the village, with over forty people attending. Included were several of the elderly sheikhs (who needed an Arabic translation). After the program we held a *japa* session with several enthusiastic Druze chanters. It’s a real *nama-hatta* village. I see a lot of potential in this Arabic preaching. Among the most devotional members of this Rama village there can soon manifest an active Arabic BBT translating, fund-raising, and printing. It’s all very exciting. I will also write to Traidasa, Harivilasa, and others interested in this field. We eventually received 270 copies of *Coming Back* in Arabic from France. They were sold very quickly at $5 (10 shekel) each. Hare Krsna.

P. S. Also this week we met a Druze businessman with book-selling connections to the Arabic countries. We’re trying to cultivate him.... Next week I have a meeting with Salman Falach in connection with the Arabic *Gita.*

October 30, 1989—DG (Haifa) to Kirtiraja dasa (Sweden), by fax:

...It was quite an exciting week. Book distribution is going nicely. This week I have a meeting with Salman Falach, Druze Minister of Education, in regards to the Arabic *Bhagavad-gita* Bhakta Salach is doing very well This week we made our Lebanese, Egyptian, Kuwait, etc., connections.

(1) Samich Natoor—A Druze bookseller with connections in all Arab countries except Syria. He has a full Hebrew set and is favorable to our movement. (He talked, half-jokingly, of opening a Hare Krsna center in Cairo.) He took ten Arabic books to check out international possibilities, e.g., he has a Druze friend in Cyprus who can get the book to Lebanon and Egypt. The book will have to be very cheap due to their economy. In Kuwait and other rich countries, the price is not the issue, but he’s not sure if there will be interest. Mr. Natoor had connections until recently with all villages in occupied territory. It’s just too hot there now, but if the situation is relieved we have no doubt that the Arab *Bhagavad-gita* will sell. We are cultivating Mr. Natoor.

(2) Falach Falach—Another Druze bookseller with connections with Lebanon. He is more advanced in devotion than Mr. Natoor. He’s seriously considering adopting Krsna consciousness in his own life. He will present the book to his Lebanese contact. He says from Lebanon the *Bhagavad-gita* can be distributed to the whole Arab world. He said clearly that if it is decided that the book can be successful in Lebanon, they will most probably print it themselves without much consideration for copyright laws at about 1.5 $ per book.

I believe that Bhakta Salach and his devotional associates could be enthusiastically engaged in BBT work—translating, fund-raising, distribution, etc. I think we should strike while the iron is hot or they will be engaged in the service of the impersonalists or materialists. How can we organize this? Also, I’ve contacted a Muslim math teacher who wants to help translate our books into Arabic I was thinking to have B. Salach initiate the project of a Hare Krsna newsletter for Arabic speakers The field of Arab preaching seems to really be opening up.

November 6, 1989—DG (Haifa) to Jayadvaita Swami:

There is quite a nice response to the Arabic *Gita.* Maybe you’ve heard that one boy from Rama, Salach, is chanting sixteen rounds/day and is helping Sri Caitanya’s *sankirtana* movement in many ways. I think he as well as others could be nicely engaged in Arabic BBT work—translating, fund-raising, and distributing books. Bhakta Salach has already sold several books in his village.

November 8, 1989—Dhira Govinda dasa (Haifa) to Jules and Pam Wolf, the author’s parents (Valley Forge, Pennsylvania):

Yes, aside from being a political reformer, Gandhi’s life and movement were based on spiritual principles. He very much treasured the *Bhagavad-gita,* spoken by Krsna five thousand years ago. Our Hare Krsna movement is based on this book. We’ve now translated the *Bhagavad-gita,* as well as several other spiritual literatures from ancient India, into Hebrew and Arabic. Maha-Laksmi and I are busy selling these books, mostly in the northern Israeli villages. My Hebrew is always becoming more fluent, and we’re both picking up a few words in Arabic. We’re planning to move to a small village called “Rama” in almost a week. It’s situated in the mountains of Northern Israel.

November 19, 1989—DG (Haifa) to Bhakta Salach Abas (Rama) \[letter translated from Hebrew\]:

Inside this envelope is the material we printed \[Arabic “Purity is the Force” flyers\]. I request that you check if it is OK to print the final copies. Please contact me at 04–660098 or 03–240774, or write to P.O. Box 48163, TA 61480. We want to print the flyer this week. There’s so much service to do for Krsna to help Srila Prabhupada save the conditioned souls. You can do a lot, and it depends on desire. Thank you for this service. You are helping me in my service to my spiritual master. *Haribol!*

November 23, 1989—Danavir dasa to ML (Haifa):

It is so nice to hear your enthusiastic spirit for distributing the Arabic books. Srila Prabhupada said that the Muslims were formerly part of the Vedic culture, but they became separate. Then they conquered so many places and peoples that the numbers grew and grew. it is the duty of the Vaisnavas to elevate them to the transcendental platform of devotional service. *Bhakti* is the natural position for all souls, despite their present dress.

It is wonderful news that you now have an Arabic young man chanting sixteen rounds, following the regulative principles, and distributing Arabic *Gitas* to whomever he meets. These accomplishments are proof of your sincerity. I pray that Lord Nrsimhadeva gives you all protection so you may continue with your most important work.

Good luck with the man who distributes Arabic books to foreign countries. This is one of the last frontiers. These people have tried to avoid Lord Caitanya’s mercy for a long time, but Lord Caitanya’s devotees’ mercy is even more irresistible.

November 23, 1989—Danavir dasa to DG (Haifa):

Your Arabic preaching is so exciting. If there is a chance for peace in the Middle East, it is possible only through Krsna consciousness on both sides. Keep up this ecstatic program and remember to never let your guard down in fighting with *maya.*

November 26, 1989—ML to Danavir dasa:

...Because we are basically concentrating on the northern part of the country we are planning to move our location up to the Druze village Rama. This happens to be the place where our *bhakta* friend Salach Abas lives. Kirtiraja Prabhu had been to the village while he was in Israel and had excitedly told us his impressions about these simple villagers who believe in reincarnation. Many people there have our books and have great appreciation and respect for the devotees.

Two weeks ago we visited Sheikh Amin Tarif, the head of the Druze. He is a very old man, in his late nineties, but has still got good memory and intelligence. We had a very interesting conversation with him for about an hour. (We taped and transcribed it. If you want, we can send you a copy.) He was very impressed with our philosophy and moral standards, and at the end concluded that “*Inshallah* (by Allah’s grace) we can work as one community and one race.”

It is amazing because Krsna is revealing many varieties of opportunities to distribute these books in Arabic. We are learning that it’s not that there is one Islam religion and *bas!* Islam is divided into so many different sects with different beliefs. About a week ago we met a Sufi sheikh. They are known as the mildest Muslims. They are more simple, austere, and they don’t deal with politics. Sheikh Adel Abed al Hamid exchanged his old abridged *Gita,* which he had purchased some eight years ago and read, with an unabridged *Gita* in Arabic. He was very friendly and gentle and wanted to keep in touch.

Then, a few days ago we met the head of the Ahmadis, which is a Muslim sect up on the Carmel mountains. His name is Muhamad Hamid. He is Indian by birth, and upon seeing Dhira Govinda Prabhu he quoted the verse, *yada yada hi dharmasya, glanir bhavati bharata.* He was confused about his philosophy, but certainly knew Krsna and said that they accept Him as a God-sent prophet that came to reestablish the principles of religion.

Later on that day we went door to door in that Ahmadi neighborhood and met very nice people that took *Gitas* in Arabic. We also met a student from an Alawi village named Ajar which is, practically speaking, in Syria. This sect, the Alawis, some of them actually believe in reincarnation. So maybe we will go there with the books.

Our Druze friend Mr. Samich Natoor, who has purchased ten books to try to sell in some Arabic countries, informed us

December 26, 1989—PG (Rama) to Danavir dasa:

I’m writing from Rama, taking a break from Tel Aviv at a very nice, clean, and Krsna conscious location: Dhira Govinda’s residence. The atmosphere is so peaceful and absorbed in KC that it tempts me to stay.

December 27, 1989—PG (Tel Aviv) to Dhira Govinda dasa (Rama):

I had such nice time in RAMA. The cool mountain air helps the mind. The KC atmosphere broadcasted from your place will surely attract the people of that village. Great hopes.

I send you back the Arabic translation of *bhajanas.* Thanks.

December 30, 1989—Danavir dasa (Dallas) to ML (Rama):

Yes, I would like a transcript of the talk you and Dhira Govinda Prabhu had with Sheikh Amin Tarif, the head of the Druze who is in his late nineties. His statement that “*Inshallah* (by Allah’s grace) we can work as one community” is significant. By preaching and living purely, phenomenal things happen. It is most encouraging, so keep preaching and living purely. Actually, all these happenings may be among the most noteworthy accomplishments in Arab missionary activity so far.

I enjoyed your description of how our Dhira Govinda Prabhu, while preaching to a biology teacher, “smashed Darwin’s theory into a primordial soup.” Not only that, but the teacher afterward complimented him and took a *Bhagavad-gita* As It Is. Furthermore the teacher said, “I have spoken with religious people who were against this theory, but they didn’t have any actual knowledge how to defeat it. This is the first time I’ve met someone religious who can defeat it scientifically with great logic and arguments.”

It is also nice that a very favorable article was printed recently in a national Arabic newspaper.

January 2, 1990—DG (Rama) to Kirtiraja dasa (Sweden):

ML and I live in Rama. The community reaction is wonderful. There are four or five young men who visit and chant *japa* regularly. We have *Bhagavatam* class every morning, usually with some Druze *bhaktas.* We have a scheduled Friday-night feast program, but we get visitors almost every evening. Our friends bring donations of *laksmi* and *bhoga.* They’re very desirous of a center. Bhakta Salach wants to work for us as a bookseller. Another seventeen-year-old boy named Woosam is chanting ten rounds per day and his parents think it’s great. I’ve heard there are three Arabic small books ready for printing. At our last Friday program some reporters came and wrote an article with pictures. There’s a big picture with me lecturing to four Druze *bhaktas.* The caption reads, “David and the Druze Krishnas.” The *bhaktas* and the villagers in general think it’s great to be known as Hare Krsna people. Sheikh Amin’s comment that he wants the Druze to work with the Krishnas “as one race” is going a long way to smooth any possible rough spots in PR. Sheikh Amin requested to meet with our world leader. It will be really nice if someone could visit and meet the Sheikh before he leaves his body. It will be on radio and in the papers. It will be a great assistance to Druze preaching for generations to come

April 20–28 thousands of Druze come from Lebanon and Israel to visit Jethro’s tomb. This is a great way to get books to Lebanon. Ideally, what I would want is to have a center here If varieties of Arabic books will be forthcoming, we can support the center as well as expand preaching projects with books. We want Arabic books.... the potential in these villages is fantastic.... Bhakta Salach has 10,000 shekels saved and says he wants to visit Vrndavana. If we could plan a holy *dhama* pilgrimage with the Druze *bhaktas,* it would be really great. Hare Krsna.

January 13, 1990—Thukura Haridasa dasa (Philadelphia) to DG (Rama):

I’m looking forward to meeting Mrs. Nurit-Dvir and reading her thesis. Ravindra-svarupa Prabhu will be in India until mid-March, but I will be on the lookout for Nurit

Your new location in the village of Rama certainly sounds auspicious. a description of book distribution successes with pictures which could be shown by other distributors around the world to help distribute more literature.

January 20, 1990—Harivilasa dasa to DG (Rama):

I received your letter dated Dec. 17, 1989 and have noted the contents carefully. I am very encouraged by your sincere efforts to preach.

There are several devotees living in Cairo. Please write them. Gaura-bhakta dasa, 5 Hassan Assem Street Flat #12, Zamalek, Cairo, Arab Republique of Egypt. I am forwarding to you photocopies of three Arabic books ready to print: We hope to print them in Europe. If you have any plans for printing more books in Israel, please inform me.

The *Bhagavad-gita* in Arabic (new edition and translation) is ready to be composed. Do you have any facilities in Israel for composing Arabic?

January 24, 1990—Dhira Govinda dasa (Rama) to Israel Ministry of the Interior (Jerusalem):

My name is David Wolf. My Israeli I.D. no. is 01–194137–4, and Israeli passport no. is 2884536. Mehenni is my friend and I am inviting him to come and stay with me in Israel.

January 27, 1990—Ananda-svarupa Swami (Cairo) to DG:

This is to officially authorize you to act as Arabic BBT manager. This authorization allows you to:

1\. Print Arabic books as needed to facilitate the preaching as per the following guidelines: (a) Arabic *Coming Back* reprint to be done in time for the upcoming Druze Festival. (b) BBT markup to be between 15% and 25%.

2\. Collect additional funds on behalf of Arabic BBT from anyone specifically interested in Arabic book production. Such funds are to be managed and accounted for separately.

I am looking forward to your cooperation and wish you success in this important branch of preaching which was so much desired by Srila Prabhupada.

February 7, 1990—Santasya to Danavir dasa:

In one library the manager who spoke with me was doing a phone invitation of many books calling by the name different novels and science fictions and modern literature. After ending his phone talk he said that he doesn’t think that except for himself there will be much demand for our books.

Then (by Lord Caitanya’s mercy) I started telling him, “I just heard you inviting \[ordering\] all these chewing the chewed literature by the kilo, but when it comes to something different than these repeated subjects of this world, something of a transcendental nature, you are hesitating to take.” He ended up with the next 10,000 years law books, Srila Prabhupada’s books.

In another library the lady manager started to open many encyclopedias to check the information I gave her about how old is the Sanskrit language and about the Vedic culture origins. She tended not to believe even one single thing I said, repeating what I knew from devotees or *Sastra.*

In the Brittanica, Sanskrit is said to be only 3,500 years old and so on and so forth—different speculations and untruths.

After finishing her “victorious” research she looked to me for an answer. I told her that this is misinformation, completely not in accordance with what I heard in disciplic succession and besides, I cannot accept the authority of an encyclopedia which wrote about Caitanya Mahaprabhu, the famous establisher of the one and different doctrine which is philosophically such a genuine spiritual conception of the Absolute Truth, to be merely a madman. Showing the *Caitanya-caritamrta* in Hebrew (*Adi-lila* in two parts), all along I prayed to change her heart, since how can we defeat mundane scholars on their low level? Suddenly she had a change of heart. She exclaimed, “Oh, there may be that some things that are really unknown to all the historians. For sure Sanskrit was spoken long ago, but they trace it back only from the writings that had been found from that time. ”

Finally convincing herself that to know the *Vedas* is the best thing, direct reading of Srila Prabhupada’s books, she took a set for the library of Hertzlia \[an upper-class Jewish suburb of Tel Aviv\].

I feel such a thrill each time a library is taking the books. Actually I’m just driving from one library to another even in different cities. Some days ago I had a dream that all libraries already have the books, and lamenting for not having more to do I started to do swimming pool managers, dealing with them exactly as with library managers. They are also happy to take the books and, in this way, no scarcity, always Sri Krsna Caitanya is sending some types of persons to engage in his glorious service.

It seems like we are going to move to Osafia (a Druze village). We are growing Tulasi-devi, and by her mercy all offenses are nullified and devotion to Lord Caitanya is established within the heart. Soon Arabic books will be printed in large quantities in Israel—mostly *Coming Back* in Druze edition. If they are those who want the books the most, why not give them facility to understand the philosophy? It seems like some friendly connections are established now with the temple in Cairo, and it is also helpful for Middle East preaching. Dhira Govinda Prabhu visited there for a week. H.H. Ananda-svarupa Maharaja is supposed to be here in April for a visit.

February 8, 1990—DG (Rama) to Harivilasa dasa (Berkeley):

Thank you for your letter dated Jan. 20, 1990. I visited Ananda-svarupa Swami and Gaura-bhakta Prabhus in Egypt for one week. Ananda-svarupa Swami is planning to visit Israel in April. We would like Gaura-bhakta to visit Israel also, but it’s not so easy to arrange a visa for him (Algerian body).

With the permission and encouragement of Ananda-svarupa Swami we are presently collecting *laksmi* to print a few thousand copies of *Coming Back* in Arabic. We are planning to have the book to the printer March 20 at the latest. Is this one of the books for which you are sending me photocopies? If so, please inform me, as we have no plates. The printing price will be much cheaper if I had plates for the book. Where are the original plates? We have a fax #–03226651. Generally speaking, we’re interested to print all of the books in Arabic. However, I don’t think it will be very difficult to import Arabic books. So, if the Arabic books can be printed in a foreign country and shipped to Israel at a significantly cheaper price, we will buy the books from a different country. Currently, it seems we can print *Coming Back* at 1–1.5 shekel (=.5–.75$) per book.

I don’t currently know of facilities for composing Arabic here in Israel, but I haven’t checked into the matter. I’m sure facilities could be arranged. Possibly Guna-avatara Prabhu would know more than me at this point. For our preaching here I would not consider a new edition of the Arabic *Gita* to be a top priority at the present time, as the old edition seems satisfactory. We would prefer new titles to be printed for distribution. But a new edition of the *Gita* with Sanskrit will also be nice. Looking forward to further cooperation with you.

February 10, 1990—DG (Rama) to Danavir dasa:

So we are looking to get a place together in the North. Any devotee who wants to be with us is welcome Isvara Krsna dasa wants to do a university preaching center, which enthuses me also. Immediately, this move will distract me from Arabic preaching. But for the Israel *yatra* as a whole, I feel it is a worthwhile (and only temporary) sacrifice.

February 12, 1990—Danavir dasa to DG (Rama):

If I’m not mistaken, we traveled through the village of Rama once in order to visit a kibbutz over the hill. Is it so? If yes, which kibbutz did we visit?

Please give my sincere appreciation to Bhakta David Harzion and family for their genuine and timely support of our preaching work in that area. Also, please thank Bhakta Salach, Bhakta Kamil, and Bhakta Maher for their continued service.... The flier and the newspaper clipping indicate you’re faring well in the P. R. department.

I would love to stay with you in Rama when I come to Israel next, although as you may have heard, it will not be possible in February as hoped. But rest assured that my desire to preach with you will not fade.

PG to Danavir dasa:

...school managers do take. We also had a very nice program in Kabul near Haifa. This month the Muslim world keeps the Ramadan fast. It’s a month of austerities—for thirty days they fast from sunrise to sunset (also from water). We find them very mellow on these days and very receptive to hear Prabhupada’s words. We present this spiritual science and they’re sure we are Muslims. They’re attracted. Here \[Israel\] is where Arab preaching is already being done. We would like to share it—I hope we’ll have visitors, many visitors this year.

Preaching to Muslims in Israel is one more point to share. Muslim intellectuals are taking books! Not the common man but doctors, lawyers, and

February 13, 1990—Danavir dasa (San Diego) to ML (Rama):

Thank you for the nice report about the preaching in village Rama. May Krsna and Rama continue to shower their mercy upon your efforts. Sheikh Farhood was definitely correct when he said that now after thirty years he has finally found some good association. Teach him nicely.

February 15, 1990—Sivananda Sena dasa (Berkeley) to DG (Rama):

Harivilasa Prabhu asked me to send the three Arabic Books which are ready to be printed, to you, and that you might have prospective donors who would like to help print them

February 24, 1990—Gaura-bhakta dasa (Cairo) to DG:

Thank you very much for the delicious *prasadam* you sent me—the visit of the *matajis* \[Israeli *matajis* stopped in Cairo on their way to India\] made me happy. You may know that I am alone since eight weeks The preaching is going nice. Most people come by the advertisement in the newspaper—Hatha-yoga lessons. I continue as before, preaching in the morning, cooking and cleaning the apt. in the afternoon. Every Friday about twelve people come regularly.

I would like to thank you a lot for the endeavor you did to arrange an authorization to come to Israel. The situation presently is such as I am obliged to stay in Cairo until the return of Maharaja because I would like to stay at least three weeks in Israel. Now I cannot close the center. So when Maharaja will be here then it is possible. Of course I will let you know before my coming. Again thanks a lot for giving me such a wonderful blessing in the form of Arab, Druze invitation preaching. KC is predicted all over the world. Druze people and Egyptians and Israelies make a very good combination for the world peace.

March 18, 1990—Trai dasa (Italy) to DG (Rama):

I understand now that you moved to this village Rama to preach more directly to the Druze people. The ways that I can help are:

1\. If you can send me detailed estimates for printing particular titles on the letterhead of the printer, I can use these to approach in different parts of the world those who have *laksmi* and get them to pay for a printing of a book. There are certain devotees living outside especially in America and Europe who are materially successful and are sympathetic to such a project.

2\. I can be the liaison with the BBT Meditt. based here in Italy to get the *laksmi* to print the books as they are ready or there in Israel or other countries.

3\. I can help you to organize the group of friends there to translate Prabhupada’s books, even if it means to pay them something and you follow the production. The idea should be to set up a group like for the Hebrew that is regularly producing books in Arabic. Perhaps I can come there to start it or if you think you can do it, we can communicate by letter.

I will try to find Ananda-svarupa Maharaja as soon as he gets back from India and see what is the situation in Egypt for printing, etc. Please let me know on these three points. The written estimates you can send me as soon as possible and I can start to circulate them. I hope I can serve you in some way in this preaching to the last great frontier on this planet—the Arabic-speaking people.

April 5, 1990—ML to Danavir dasa:

We are preparing to move to the new temple in the Druze village Isfiya, which is very close to Haifa. Dhira Govinda and Parama-guru started to distribute the Arabic *Coming Back* today, which I think is going to be a huge success.

I am enclosing herewith the transcription of the conversation we had with Sheikh Amin Tarif.

April 13, 1990—DG (Rama) to Trai dasa (Italy):

Thank you for your inspiring letter of 3/18/90. In response to your points:

(1) Harivilasa and Sivananda Sena Prabhus have both sent me letters indicating that they will send me copies of Arabic books for printing. I have not yet received the copies of the books though. As soon as I do I can then get detailed estimates for printing on the letterhead of the printer. The *Coming Backs* and the Arabic *Gitas* are selling very nicely.

(2) On *sankirtana,* I’m casually asking if anyone is interested to translate Srila Prabhupada’s books in Arabic. There definitely is interest, especially among the Druze. One boy, a graduate in engineering, is currently working on *Perfect Questions,* and then plans to start *Krsna* book. Here in Rama, a good friend of ours is a teacher of Arabic in the high school. He’s willing to check the translation from a literary point of view. I believe we could definitely organize an Arabic translation team here. Of course, they’re translating from Hebrew. We may also be able to find a fluent English speaker to do the translation if we made some search. It will be nice if you come and visit. My time is spread in many areas at present. I’m on *sankirtana* every day. I could basically follow the production. It will be ecstatic to see Prabhupada’s books produced and printed in Arabic on a regular basis. It would seem to be overdue, as it’s such a major language on the planet Do you think it would be cheaper to print in Italy?

On April 24–26 there is a big Druze festival in Israel, and many will be coming from Syria and Lebanon. I think it will be a great opportunity for book distribution. You are cordially invited if you can come to Israel at that time. I’ll be in Rama until at least June.

April 13, 1990—Ananda-svarupa Swami (Amsterdam) to DG (Rama):

As requested I am enclosing two letters for the Druze sheikhs. I do not know the name of the second sheikh, so I left it blank.

I want to meet the Druze sheikhs, so just make the arrangements and contact me in Cairo. I’ll be a little busy initially and I am expecting visitors during the first three weeks of May. I was delayed in India, discussing all sorts of plans for Arab preaching and book production. We got a $10,000 donation from the BBT for printing and hopefully we’ll see *Isopanishad, Readings in Vedic Literature,* and *Consciousness, The Missing Link* later this year.

News from Cairo are good. Gaura-bhakta is doing well and is eager to visit Israel as well. We’ll arrange it later in the year

Please let me know everything as I am counting on your wonderful enthusiasm

April 24, 1990—Nanda-kisora dasa (Fiji) to DG (Rama):

I beg to acknowledge receipt of your letter dated 18th Nov., 1989 wherein you’ve requested for our periodical named the “Krishna Sun,” which features an article about the Druze preaching in Israel.

Enclosed, please find some copies of the periodical you requested. I hope you find them useful in your preaching.

If you find some time, please write to me about all your preaching activities. I’m sure that would be very inspiring for all the devotees here. Furthermore, please do not hesitate to write if I can be of any assistance to you.

April 25, 1990—DG (Rama) to Ananda-svarupa Swami:

The $10,000 donation from the BBT for Arabic printing is ecstatic news. I’m looking forward to distribute the books I’ve received a letter from Trai dasa Prabhu, indicating that he’s ready to arrange some financial help for both translating and printing Arabic books. There’s one boy here, a university graduate, who’s currently translating *Perfect Questions* on a voluntary basis. We have a good Druze friend who is an English teacher and has expressed enthusiasm to translate Srila Prabhupada’s books into Arabic. He would require some sort of payment though. I’ve heard from Guna-avatara Prabhu that Harivilasa Prabhu offered to pay the plane fare of someone to U.S.A. to translate books in Arabic and stay in Berkeley. For the price of the plane ticket this English teacher would probably translate at least one, if not more, Maha big books in Arabic. Wouldn’t it be great to see *Krsna* book and First Canto appear in the Arabic language? As far as printing, you’ve probably seen by now the new edition of *Coming Back* with quotes by Kahlil Gibran and a picture of Kamal Jumbalat. It’s selling very nicely among the Arabs here in Israel. Where do you plan to do future printings? *Coming Back* cost about $0.75 per copy. I think such a book could be done for about $0.45 here in Israel if I was more personally involved in the printing process. That is, I gave the book to the printer and got the lowest price possible. If I were to go personally to paper suppliers, etc., I could get a lower printing price. Also, if the book already has plates, as *Coming Back* didn’t, the book would be cheaper to print. Trai dasa asked me to send him detailed estimates of printing costs on the letterhead of the printer and he’ll work on funding the printing. We’ve made a nice contact here for printing in Lebanon. Gaura-bhakta Prabhu will tell you more As far as our residential arrangements, we’re in Rama until about June 10th

April 30, 1990—ML to Danavir dasa:

We received a very encouraging letter from HDG Ravindra-svarupa Prabhu in which he said, “I am very encouraged to hear about your very interesting preaching among the Druze. During our big protest march and Ratha-yatra in Washington, D.C. on April 1, I met a Druze on the Capitol Mall who was very enlivened to see us. He said that he had heard that their leaders were very interested in and favorable toward Krsna consciousness. You can see that word is getting around. Keep up the good work.”

Gaura-bhakta Prabhu, a devotee born in Algeria who is serving now in Egypt, came for a visit and stayed with us in Rama. He was very impressed with how the Arabs in Israel are so open to Krsna consciousness, especially the Druze, who believe in reincarnation and have many similarities with Vedic culture. Also, during his stay, many auspicious things happened. Santasya Prabhu gave two full English sets to Muslim teachers living in nearby villages (this is after they have taken a Hebrew set last year), and there was the grand annual Druze festival in the tomb of the prophet Jethro. This is the biggest happening in the year, and many sheikhs came from Lebanon as well as the occupied territories in the Golan Heights. These sheikhs proved to be very nice and attracted to our books, and in one day the devotees sold twenty-five Arabic *Gitas,* plus many small books in Arabic (specifically *Coming Back,* which is now printed in Arabic). This is very encouraging because some of these books will cross the border and reach Lebanon and other countries where the *sankirtana* movement of Lord Caitanya has not penetrated as of yet.

May 11, 1990—Danavir dasa (Los Angeles) to ML (Rama):

The conversation with the sheikh is well done.

P.S. Just received your letter dated April 30, 1990 along with a note from Dhira Govinda Prabhu and the article “Living in Rama.” Things really sound wonderful there. The comment from Ravindra-svarupa Prabhu regarding the Druze phenomenon is significant.

Dhira Govinda, thank you for the nice description of the new center in Osafia; yes I would love to come there and preach with you. Let us see what Krsna has in mind. Thank you for doing this service, which is very satisfying to Prabhupada.

May 14, 1990—PG to Danavir dasa:

. The preaching here is so ecstatic that I became attached. Going door-to-door in the new Jewish settlements in the north engaged in telling Krsna’s stories from silk paintings, giving books, and being dependent on the mercy of the Lord. And also going to Muslim villages in the month of Ramadan—a holy month of fasting daily—and the best, the association with the Druze in *sankirtana,* being absorbed in transcendental topics as reincarnation, creation and annihilation, *avataras,* etc. They know what to ask.

DG to Danavir dasa:

Parama-guru, Maha-Laksmi, and I have been based in Rama for about three months. Our general program is *sadhana* and book distribution. We’re very enlivened. Within about two weeks we’re planning to move to Osafia, outside Haifa, with Isvara Krsna Prabhu. A friendly Druze English teacher is constructing the downstairs part of his house to rent to us. It will be quite spacious with only the five of us (plus Jahnavi). It’s about 150 sq. meters. He (Salim) designed the interior according to our plan. So it’s built like a temple-*asrama,* with separation for men and women, and a nice-sized temple room. Maybe we can make some devotees to fill up the place As ML wrote, we’ve been having some really ecstatic times recently with the Druze. Maybe you can visit.

May 25, 1990—ML to Danavir dasa:

Dhira Govinda and Parama-guru Prabhus have met our first Druze *bhaktin.* She is following the four regulative principles and has read the five books in her set. She also happens to be the fiancee of Maher Faraj, one of our best friends in the village who is also inclined to Krsna consciousness.

Isvara Krsna and Radha-Madhava Prabhus have already moved to Osafia and the place is taking shape slowly. We are planning to move in on the 10th of June. Devahuti and Guna-avatara Prabhus will stay with us during July–August until their departure for India.

While writing to you about all these wonderful preaching matters I hear over my head the loud noise of passing military

May 30, 1990—DG (Rama) to Rezek Zedan (Rama) (translated from Hebrew):

Hare Krsna. It was very nice to visit you at your house a few days ago. We’ll be in Rama until June 10, and we have a morning program every morning at 5:00 a.m. You are respectfully invited. I’ll be happy to see you there. With all sincerity I want to encourage you to continue on your Krsna conscious path. Only Krsna can save us from the material world and all of its suffering conditions. The strength to defeat the temptations of *maya* comes from the chanting of the *maha-mantra—*Hare Krsna, Hare Krsna, Krsna Krsna, Hare Hare/ Hare Rama, Hare Rama, Rama Rama, Hare Hare. Also, strength comes from following the regulative principles. Srila Prabhupada recommended a minimum of sixteen rounds every day for devotees who are serious about their service to Krsna and about becoming free from material entanglements. I was very happy to hear that you are reading Prabhupada’s books regularly. Please continue to study these books about Krsna more deeply. As far as practical matters, we have some work to do on our new temple in Osafia. We thought you might be interested to help in this regard. Hare Krsna.

June 20, 1990—Danavir dasa (Los Angeles) to ML (Osafia):

You mentioned that the situation is very tense in Israel, and a war is predicted. Under the circumstances, I think that Dhira Govinda, yourself, and the other devotees should give careful consideration to your own safety.

It is very nice to hear about the first Druze *bhaktin* who is following the regulative principles and reading Srila Prabhupada’s books. Kindly give her my best wishes for wonderful advancement in devotional service.

June 26, 1990—DG (Osafia) to Thukura Haridasa dasa (Philadelphia):

I’ve recently met with Nurit and her husband here in Israel. They enjoyed the association at ISKCON Philadelphia very much If you’re interested, we can provide many *sankirtana* stories from a variety of devotees. Practically every day some amazing thing is happening. In the May “World *Sankirtana* News-letter” there is a description of the festival where all the sheikhs took books. As far as pictures, we’re not set up in any sort of professional way to take pictures. I have the pictures that you saw last summer; i.e., a few Druze holding the set of books after they purchased, as well as some pictures from some public programs we did in villages. Also, there are the pictures that appeared in *IWR* and *BTG,* of Sheikh Amin and Salman Falach, the Education Minister.

June 26, 1990—DG (Osafia) to Satyaraja dasa (New York):

Isvara Krsna Prabhu has informed me that you have telephoned Osafia in connection with material for your new book. Among influential rabbis here in Israel, the best contact I can think of is a Rabbi Lippel, of the Hasidim (I believe the Bressler sect). Rabbi Lippel is the founder of the “Jerusalem Institute of Interreligious Affairs,” created mainly for the purpose of public relations for the religious community. Lippel (I believe he is also a Dr. and/or a professor) printed an annual calender marking important dates for all of the major religions that populate the holy land. In 1986 Danavir Prabhu met with Lippel. We have the tape of the conversation. I wouldn’t describe it as overwhelmingly favorable, but at least the Rabbi was friendly and respectful toward the devotees. Since 1986, we have not recontacted Lippel, although it may be fruitful if we did.

As far as Muslims, we have a very nice Sufi sheikh who has been a friend of the Krsna consciousness movement for about ten years. He speaks English also. If you want to do work with the Muslims, Israel may be a good place to start, as the Muslims are generally much more humble and approachable than in countries where they are the controllers. As far as the Druze, what can I say? I really think something amazing can develop here. Their religious hierarchy is expecting an incarnation of the Supreme Logic Potency of the Godhead (something like a *Saktyavesa-avatara*) to appear in the present age for the purpose of uniting all religions of the world. I’m sure if we continue to infuse the Druze society with books, they will realize that Prabhupada is the pure soul for whom they are awaiting.

June 26, 1990—DG (Osafia) to Madhusevita dasa (Italy):

I feel that many, if not all, aspects of Arabic BBT work can be executed here. Especially, many Druze are spontaneously translating the books into Arabic even without our asking.

June 26, 1990—DG (Osafia) to Ananda-svarupa Swami (Cairo and Amsterdam):

I feel there is full potential here for all aspects of Arabic BBT work. If I may suggest that “*Prabhupada* book” is the next book translated and printed into Arabic, then *Bhagavatam,* etc. Hare Krsna.

June 26, 1990—DG (Osafia) to Harivilasa dasa (Berkeley):

I really feel something big can happen here. But I’m so unqualified. Please help. I feel confident to say that there is potential to set up a full Arabic BBT here. Many Druze are voluntarily translating parts of the books, without us even asking.

P. S. Enclosed is the copy of *Coming Back* we printed (5000 copies). On the inside of the title page are some quotes from Gibran, and on page 35 is the picture of Jumbalat. Hare Krsna.

July 1, 1990—Satyaraja dasa (New York) to DG (Osafia):

Thank you for your letter and essay. My new book *Om Shalom: Judaism and Krishna Consciousness* is finally at the printer and we will have copies by July 19. The book is 224 pages of conversation with Rabbi Jacob Shimmel, and we get in-to some very deep areas. I hope you will find the volume useful for your preaching.

I would like that tape of dialogue with Rabbi Lippel, and please send other such nectar. I will definitely send you a sample copy of my book upon its release. Anxiously awaiting your reply and the Lippel tape

July 8, 1990—Jayadvaita Swami (San Diego) to DG (Osafia):

Thank you for your letter of June 24, along with the accounts of the preaching work there. Yes, I very much appreciated receiving them. I’d be grateful if you can continue to send me news from time to time.

I spoke with Harivilasa Prabhu today. We both think that if you can get Arabic translation work done in Israel, that might be very helpful. Printing is probably best done outside Israel, because of the high Israeli prices. But translation we can do wherever we have translators. So I’d suggest you try to get some sample translation work done there, and you can work with Harivilasa Prabhu to see about checking the quality of the translation, and so on.

Are you in touch with Trai dasa also? What’s going on from his side in Arabic book production?

Your preaching there sounds like it’s going on very nicely. Certainly Krsna will give you His blessings more and more.

July 12, 1990—Bhakta Jeffrey Rudick (Badger, CA) to DG (Osafia):

But I mean it sincerely, seems like you’ve been quite expert, deft and adroit (bringin’ them odd words out for an airing) at dialoguing with these venerable fellows, piquing their interest and sustaining their natural connection with the principles and wellsprings of Krsna consciousness. That’s wonderful work you’re doing, and it sounds darned interesting for ya’ too! Of course if I catch a picture of you with ample white beard and pillbox hat we gonna get worried!

July 15, 1990—PG to Danavir dasa:

All glories to Sri Sri Guru and Gauranga!! My meditation is to support the Osafia project/ temple through book distribution. Everybody that comes in contact with it becomes appreciative.

The community here is inquisitive and some brave pioneers come to our Friday programmes regularly. As we find out the secrets of the Druze religion as being very similar to ours, They are fascinated and call us Druze from India. The buyers of the books accept us like this, my feeling is that meetings with ISKCON leaders on some level can be arranged to produce nice cooperation, understanding and PR.

One small service I could offer to the Israeli *yatra* is that I shoot these days a video film presenting the preaching to the Druze. We have interviews with sheikhs talking about how similar the two philosophies are, some practitioners and readers and village scenes. We have half the material we need according to the script we did. Some more interviews and editing are standing between us and the compilation of the film.

August 10, 1990—DG (Osafia) to Ravanari dasa:

We’re eagerly awaiting your arrival to Israel. Since we’ve been distributing the Arab *Gita* many of our friends have suggested, “Why don’t you bring Ravech Yunis to Israel?” Hoping this meets you well. Hare Krsna.

August 16, 1990—DG (Osafia) to Danavir dasa:

Thank you for your encouraging letter of July 9, 1990. Krsna continues to shower His mercy upon this fallen soul by giving me daily the opportunity to go out dressed as a Hare Krsna devotee and directly present Srila Prabhupada’s books to people, who generally give donations in return. I’d like to continue this program as long as possible. Mainly, I’m distributing *Bhagavad-gita* and *Coming Back* in Arabic. With only these books, there’s not too much area left to do here in Israel. My main concern now is that more Arabic books will be produced, so I can distribute them.

We’re just after an ecstatic two-day festival in Osafia for Janmastami and Srila Prabhupada’s Vyasa-puja. More than fifty devotees and guests attended, including Guna-avatara and Devahuti (who organized the cooking of more than 108 preparations), Nitya-siddha and Saci Devi, Suddha-sattva, Meyer, Ilana Gordon with her husband and daughter, Murari Caitanya and family, and many other friendly people from both the Jewish and Druze communities.

I would describe the Janmastami festival as overwhelming. For most of our non-Druze guests it was their first time here. Although they had heard about the place, still there was some conception that we’re residing in a grass hut amid the terrorists, or at least friends of the terrorists. So the festival was pervaded by an atmosphere of amazement at what a nice place we had found—large house with spacious temple room in the country with fresh air and nice neighbors who really like Hare Krsna (allowing us to have a rip-roaring *kirtana* past midnight). Some wanted to join just by seeing the temple.... The Druze were also overwhelmed to have such an event occur in a remote corner of their remote village. In Rama and in Osafia until now, the programs would generally consist of Maha-Laksmi and me doing our best to host a *Gita* class, *bhajana,* and some *prasadam*. Also, the Druze, possessing traditional etiquette, would generally be appreciative though reserved about taking *prasadam*. Now, when they saw our respectable Jewish guests (whom they admire in many ways) gorging themselves uninhibitedly, they wholeheartedly joined in. Similarly with the *kirtana*. Our landlord was practically in tears (of joy) that his house could be used in such a nice way. Even though we currently have no phone line and the place is difficult for people to get to, still, so many came. Krsna’s mercy. We feel this festival really established Osafia temple

Tulasi-devi is growing very nicely and we worship her daily. Just today a very favorable newspaper article appeared in a Hebrew paper, with color pictures, about Hare Krsna in Osafia. Previously, this paper had only written trashy stuff about Hare Krsna. The Jewish community is also favorably impressed that the Druze are so receptive to Hare Krsna. Sheikh Amin recently won the Israeli peace prize, as that personality who is doing the most for peace. Of course, it’s all mundane politics, but the point is that the Druze are respected by mainstream Israel, unlike other Arabs. So my general activity is to stay back in the morning, dealing with management and performing *sadhana,* and in the afternoon and evening I drive to some village for book distribution. If new titles are regularly produced I can see distributing like this, maintaining and expanding, without limit. There’s a great market. I’m sure the books will produce devotees, what to speak of a deeply favorable impression towards Krsna consciousness

August 28, 1990—Ravanari dasa to DG (Osafia):

It was a pleasure to talk with you on the phone and to receive your kind letter. I was enthused and touched by your sincere endeavor to push on Lord Caitanya’s mission in this troubled part of the world, which very much needs to taste the mercy of Lord Caitanya.

September 3, 1990—Madhusevita dasa (Italy) to DG (Osafia):

After having sent a brief answer to Isvara Krsna’s letter in which a footnote was addressed to you, I received a fax from Jayadvaita Swami reporting portions of a letter you sent to him. He is urging me to become involved more conspicuously in the Israeli BBT and the Arabic book production: so let us try to develop some working relationship to please Srila Prabhupada.

You’ll be glad to know that 5,000 *Isopanishads* and 5,000 *Consciousness, The Missing Link* in Arabic language are in print in Hong Kong. Let me know how many you think you can use in Israel. This printing is totally funded by the Italian BBT.

I knew Ravanari in India ’74, don’t know if he remembers me: anyway we should let him know we are ready to support his translating work in Israel, but we want two things: a program containing the titles he wants to translate and an estimated budget

Madhusevita dasa (Italy) to DG (Osafia):

1,000 Arabic books are in print in Hong Kong and we expect the delivery in a month’s time.

About Ravanari, Jayadvaita Swami and I had an exchange of views and the general inclination is to let him go directly to Israel to translate and preach. Naturally he would be subsidized by our division of the BBT, and we should work out his translation schedule together on the basis of the possibilities of distribution for different titles.

September 4, 1990—Hrdayananda Maharaja (Los Angeles) to DG (Osafia):

I am inspired by your plans Thank you very much for your dedicated and innovative devotional service.

September 5, 1990—Danavir dasa to DG (Osafia):

I really enjoyed your description of the wonderful festival at Osafia and all the devotees that were present. I wish I could have been there also. I’m looking to visiting Israel on my way to India if the political situation allows. So in conclusion thank you for your wonderful determination to preach amidst a difficult spot with many troubles and I hope that

September 14, 1990—Gaura-bhakta dasa (France) to DG (Osafia):

P. S. Enclosed *Sri Isopanishad* Arabic translation from Ravanari. I had it since twelve years. Please print it and distribute it to all Arabs living in Israel and Prabhupada will be very happy. May Srila Prabhupada bless you.

September 14, 1990—Sivarama Swami (Bhaktivedanta Manor) to DG (Osafia):

Your idea that Ravanari Prabhu should come and preach by translating into Arabic is certainly a good idea. I would hope that the local GBC will take it seriously and take all attention to this important preaching.
