But the opposite has actually happened. The sheikhs were charmed by Tusti-mohana Prabhu, and they keep telling me how intelligent and nice he is. They are in love with Gopakumara Prabhu. Gopakumara is doing valuable service as a good, fired-up *brahmacari*. He is tireless. I have had to correct him ony a couple of times, once about translating only what the speaker says, and the other for being too pushy in his preaching to the sheikhs. But each time he has responded very well. The fact is, they like him very much and seem happy that he is living a devotee’s life.

I don’t know if I mentioned this, but on the day of the reception in the temple, they also gave small gifts to both Tusti-mohana and Gopakumara. They especially mentioned that they were happy that they were both practicing Hare Krsna.

Here are a few things the sheikhs have said since being here:

1\. While accepting *maha-prasadam* from Jayapataka Swami: “We think that all our problems have now been solved simply by accepting this sanctified food from your hands.”

2\. When discussing the Ganga: “We are very eager to go to the Ganges, because we have heard that simply by touching the water to our heads we will be freed of all sins.”

3\. When discussing the visit here: “The thing that has impressed us the most is the humility of the devotees. They are all so humble and gentle. We have a lot to learn from them.”

When we were on the boat, they had bottles which they filled with Ganges water and happily passed around to sprinkle on their heads.

So far, the trip is going fine. But this was, in my opinion, the easiest part, being that it has all taken place within ISKCON over Gaura-Purnima and in such a spiritually surcharged atmosphere. Tomorrow we go to Puri for our appointment with the king.

By the way, I am worried about the Delhi temple, as I have written fifteen letters over the past two months to various persons there and received no reply at all. Not a single reply. We are scheduled to be in Delhi on March 31st. It would be nice to have made arrangements for the Druze’s arrival.

I took photographs with a digital camera my sister lent me. Whenever I get a good computer connection, I’ll try to send some.

Your servant,

Vijnana dasa Vanacari

March 11

We left Mayapur at 5:30 a.m. for Calcutta, where we would catch a plane to Bhubhaneswar. Jayapataka Swami would be traveling on the same flight. We arrived in Bhubhaneswar around 12 p.m. Bhakta-rupa and Vanamali Prabhus were at the airport to greet us. Dr. Patrani, who is a member of Parliament, was there to greet Jayapataka Maharaja. Therefore, he also had the opportunity to meet the Druze, who in turn took the opportunity to have their photograph taken with him. Bhakta-rupa had cars ready to take us to Puri, and we left immediately.

I was in a vehicle with Gopakumara and one of the elder Druze sheikhs, Fuad Aburukun. He didn’t like the driving, and I heard later that the other sheikhs hadn’t liked it either They kept telling their drivers to slow down. Of course, we know that they would have had more success if they had asked an elepahant to dance the lead part in *Swan Lake.* Anyway, the sheikh and I started talking through Gopakumara’s translation and ended up having a long discussion about Vedic culture—kings, family life, the duties of husband and wife, etc. In the end, we exchanged stories. I told some Gopal Bhan stories that Prabhupada told, and he told some stories from his culture. It was a wonderful experience—almost like traveling with another devotee, discussing the principles of Krsna consciousness, and becoming enlivened by one another’s realizations. Fuad is generally quiet, and had hardly said anything since he’s been here, but now I know he is enlivened and happy to have come.

We arrived in Puri at about 2:00 p.m. At 7:00, we went to meet the king. He had invited a few important citizens from Puri to attend. After the introductions were over, the King asked Sheikh Hussein questions about the Druze. I jotted down some notes from their discussion. Here is a rough transcription:

**Sheikh Hussein (SH):** Thank you for spiritual qualities, and we thank the great scholars in this palace. I would like to call attention to the great service you’re doing for the temple. We are small in Middle East, and we are only ten percent of total population in Israel. But we are rich in faith, and from this principle we came so that our cultures can meet. We know that we exist in the universe from the time of creation and from that time which is more than one hundred million years. We know that many incarnations have come and we came to India searching for this. Since the last incarnation we are known as Druze. We know that there are spiritual practitioners everywhere. When we were visiting various temples, we saw that there are many symptoms similar to our own culture, and thus we are convinced that we are part of this spiritual family. We are also coming from a holy land where many prophets came. In our culture, the grandmother says to the children that I hope I may live long enough to come to the gate of heaven. So in order to fulfill the desires of our ancestors, we have come to India to find the gate to heaven. Although we are not kings, we request your Majesty to come down to our level and to visit our humble community. We wish to maintain this great brotherhood that we have started.

**Gajapati Maharaja (GM):** What is the connection between the Druze and the Christians, Jews, and Muslims?

**SH:** Our belief is that all the processes to reach the Absolute Truth are the same. So in this sense their is some affinity. Religions are like the appetite. All give some knowledge of God, but some give more, according to the capacity of the persons.

**GM:** Did the Druze tradition exist before Christ?

**SH:** Long before. Under different names.

**GM:** What indications do you have that the roots are from India?

**SH:** The duty of all Druze is to accept that the universe is at least 340 million years old. We also believe that the cosmic manifestation has existed longer than that. We have seen that the scriptures of India are the closest to our conception

Unfortunately, my notebook was small and I ran out of paper at this point, but from memory, Sheikh Hussein made an important point regarding the Deities. He said that although in the Druze religion Deity worship is forbidden, they can understand that it may be due to time, place, and circumstance. They are willing to accept that the process itself may be suitable in another time, place, or circumstance. This point had also come up in discussions with Jayapataka Maharaja. Maharaja had explained the nine processes of devotional service, and the Druze admitted that Deity worship had to be included as one of the ways of approaching the Supreme.

The Druze are definitely broadminded and eager to learn more about our philosophy.

The meeting with the King went on until about 10:30 p.m. About halfway through, Jayapataka Swami arrived with a few disciples. This was even more enlivening. I had to leave in order to arrange Jagannatha *prasadam* for the evening, so I missed part of the discussion, but some of Maharaja’s disciples videotaped the whole thing from the time that they arrived. The Druze also taped it.

At the end, Gajapati Maharaja made a wonderful presentation about the predicament of the soul implicated in material affairs. He guided them step by step through the development of the soul’s desire to reunite with the Supreme and the Supreme’s assistance via *avataras* and His representatives. He concluded by presenting them with copies of the *Bhagavad-gita* as the essence of Vedic literature. They were all totally blissful upon receiving the gifts. They later commented that they really liked his final presentation.

The other day, the sheikhs asked me about the meaning of the *maha-mantra.* I explained that Krsna is the all-attractive, Rama is the source of all pleasure, and Hare as the Lord’s energy. Thus the mantra means, “O energy of the Lord, O Lord, please help me to engage in Your service.” They loved my explanation and asked that it be written down so they could take it with them. It is interesting to note that a few of them are attentively taking notes at all the meetings.

March 12th was a day of rest, as the trip has been quite hectic since we arrived. In the evening, Citesvara came to visit them, and they recieved an introduction into *Tantra,* etc.

Tusti-mohana Krsna Prabhu, Jayapataka Maharaja’s secretary, has been an invaluable help. He is Druze, and speaks fluent Arabic. His family is from Venezuela, but he has spent time in Lebanon. He is quite philosophical and knows a lot about the Druze philosophy. All the sheikhs like him very much. He is here with us in Puri, and will be staying with us through the 13th, even though Maharaja is leaving.

Your servant,

Vijnana dasa Vanacari

From Phalguna dasa:

Dear Dhira Govinda Prabhu

I am happy to tell you that the Druze were here and seemed happy. I had the fortune to be present at some of the important meetings and activities.

The day after Gaura-Purnima they met with Jayapataka Swami and Maha-visnu Swami. The purpose of the meeting was to ask questions. The Druze presented intelligent questions that the Maharajas answered expertly. At a certain point, the Druze expressed that they were so enlivened that they didn’t mind missing anything else in their schedules, as long as they could keep listening to this philosophy. They also said that Mayapur had changed their hearts and approach to God.

Something interesting took place. Jayapataka Swami presented a *pavitra* (a five-color cloth offered to Nrsimhadeva) to one of the leaders. They were surprised when they saw that the five colors were the same five colors their most important symbol (a flag with five colors) possesses. They inquired about the meaning of these five colors, and Jayapataka Swami said that he didn’t know their meaning, but that in our culture, there are also five truths: the Panca-tattva.

I think that the service you have rendered in your preaching in Israel has been wonderful.

Your servant

Phalguna dasa

Here is the report for March 15 and 16:

March 15, \[Vrndavana\]

At 7:45 a.m., just after *guru-puja,* the Druze came into the temple. They proceeded to offer respects to Srila Prabhupada and each of the Deities. At each altar they offered flowers and incense. Then they were seated to Srila Prabhupada’s right. Prabhavisnu Swami, Bir Krsna Swami, Dhananjaya, Kadamba-kanana Swami, and Mahaman Prabhu sat with them.

Sheikh Hussein spoke first. Here is a rough transcription of his talk:

**Sheikh Hussein:** We have never felt God’s presence as much as we do today. We bless you all with the names of Hare Krsna, Hare Rama. We are coming from a place that is also considered holy. We have the holy name there, and the prophets. We are coming from a group of people who are believers in one God. We also respect all His prophets and messengers, who bring good messages for the human race. We know, according to our own understanding, that these beliefs do not belong to a certain race or religion. Therefore, when a man has elevated himself regarding knowledge, he can know what is the real truth. Like this he becomes more knowledgeable and knows his situation regarding God. Because God is existing in every one of us and all over the universe.

We know that our religion is spread all over the world and in every country, because the father of the religion created the religion when the universe was created. We consider ourselves part of this religion, although there is some difference in the rituals. The leaders and spiritual seekers of our group—for example—Kamal Jumbalat—he also came to India and found so many similarities between our belief and the Hare Krsna belief. Formerly we were getting this knowledge via reading the books, but now we get more realization by meeting the devotees. That is why we have found some embassies of this belief in our village. And we are trying to make some benefit to the society with the help of Yamuna, Vijnana Prabhu, and Bhibisana. That’s why we were crossing such a long area only in order to come here to you. We hope the inspiration or illumination of God is falling upon the head of each one of us here. We hope that by our prayers, there will be peace between the many races of the world.

When we are meeting here with the people, we are getting the realization that the Druze are everywhere. and among this people also. We met with people from the countries of the forefathers of the Druze community. It’s predicted in our books that the final teachings of our religion will conclude in India.

Whatever we are going to speak here is much less than we are feeling and appreciating toward you. That is why I am really appreciating all the spiritual leaders who are here, and the devotees. I would like to present the group of people that came with us from our place. Some of them are religious and are performing all the rituals, and some are dealing with culture and doing so much benefit from their community. Since we stepped on the holy land of Mayapur, we felt that we were coming home. As we said there is no distance between us. If that is so, I am inviting you all to come to our village too. I am getting much inspiration from my Godbrothers here, and I would like to finish now by telling everyone Hare Krsna, Hare Rama.

**Bir Krsna Goswami:** I am feeling that you are our Godbrothers and am feeling inspired to go visit you all. I would like to present you with the *Bhagavad-gita.*

(At this point, Maharaja presented the group with a *Bhagavad-gita.)*

**Prabhavisnu Swami:** Some years ago I came across a book written by one of our ISKCON devotees named Dhira Govinda dasa. I think that you must know that devotee, because he wrote a book called *Krsna, Israel and the Druze.* I was fascinated by this book, and couldn’t stop reading it. I read it from cover to cover and was very inspired. In the Vedic culture it is said that a guest should be treated as if God himself has come. Srila Prabhupada was always very keen that we accord a warm and cordial welcome to all visitors. I thank you all very, very much for coming, and I feel honored to have had the opportunity to have met all of you. Thank you and Hare Krsna

**Kadamba-kanana Swami:** For the last few years we have several times heard about the Druze community. We heard from devotees in Israel how nice the relationship was with them. We heard about many similarities between our philosophies. We heard that the famous example of *Bhagavad-gita,* where the living entity is in a chariot drawn by five horses, is also part of the Druze philosophy. By hearing all this, we have developed love for the Druze and very much desired to meet you personally. I feel that this meeting in person has added to our relationship. Because when we heard you speak, we could have a firsthand expersience of your genuine spiritual dedication—How there is one God, who is present in all of our hearts and throughout the universe, that this one God is the Supreme Father. You spoke of brotherhood. So by your coming here to our temple and our holy place, Sri Vrndavana, we are getting an experience of that brotherhood. I think it is truly an historical time. Because here today there are many people from many different countries around the world. Just looking around I see devotees from England, America, Ireland, Finland, Italy, Russia, Hungary, and so many other countries. I have named only a few. All of us will take home this mood of reconfirmed relationship with the Druze.

Because I don’t think that there is anyone here who is not genuinely inspired that you have come to speak and be with us, to share with us, so I want to thank you very much. I would request Dhananjaya, who is the host for the Druze, to please come.

**Dhananjaya Prabhu:** First, I would like to thank you very much for taking so much time and trouble to come to Sri Vrndavana-dhama. You mentioned that when you arrived in Mayapur you felt that you were coming home. I am also praying that you will feel the same way coming to Sri Vrndavana-dhama.

Srila Prabhupada used to always tell me that Sri Vrndavana and Sri Mayapur are the real homes of all devotees. The Vrndavana influence is that you also feel much peace and happiness to be here, because this is the land of Lord Krsna. Krsna tells Arjuna in *Bhagavad-gita* that He is the father of all living entities. No matter what community we come from—I was brought up as a Christian in my country, but actually, we are all brothers and sisters of the same Father. There is only one God. And the real reason that there are problems with different religious groups is due to skin disease, identifiying with the skin that covers the body. Spiritually, we are actually all the same. We may be born in various countries and have different rituals, but there is only one God, just as there is only one sun in the sky.

The special purpose of Sri Vrndavana is that actually Krsna, God, reveals Himself in His personality of Godhead here. He is the Supreme Personality of Godhead. Therefore, Sri Caitanya Mahaprabhu declared that every community in the world should call on the nectarean names of God. Those names are the Hare Krsna *maha-mantra.* We are all looking forward to working together and chanting together, and in this way going back to Godhead. \[At this point, Dhananjaya raised his hands and chanted the whole *mantra,* and the Druze raised their hands and chanted with him.\] Thank you very much.

**Mahaman Prabhu:** Today we are very happy to have all of you distinguished spiritual leaders. We have been eagerly waiting for this day. When Vijnana Prabhu sent me a message that you would be coming here, I felt so happy to hear the news. I would like to say just a few words before asking the distinguished guests to again be engaged in chanting by raising the hands.

\[Unclear\] Haridasa Thukura, who is the *namacarya* and given the responsibility of chanting the holy name. You have just come from Mayapur where there is the Chand Kazi’s tomb. He too was influenced by Caitanya Mahaprabhu to not disturb the chanting of Hare Krsna. \[Mahaman Prabhu asked everyone to chant, the Druze again raised their hands to chant loudly.\] We are actually not Hindus, Muslims, or Christians but spirit souls. We are all eternal servants of God. I feel again so much happiness that you are here, and hope that your stay is comfortable.

**Sheikh Hussein:** \[Beginning unclear\] These five elements are indicated by the five different colors, and you can see these colors everywhere. That is why I want to present you a humble present that signifies these five elements and gives some meaning of the five understandings. \[At this point, Sheikh Hussein presented a gift—a plaque to the temple authorities and all the senior devotees, including the *sannyasis,* etc. He also gave a few smaller gifts to the leaders who were present.\]

After this, Vedavyasapriya Swami mentioned that they had heard that the Druze had chanted a song with Hare Krsna in it when entering Mayapur. He said that the devotees here are eager to hear that song, and if they could chant it, the devotees would be very happy. So the group of Druze obliged by chanting their Hare Krsna song. I will try and get the translation and send it.

At 11:00 a.m. they went to the Vrndavana Research Institute and saw the museum there. Then they met with some of the leading citizens of Vrndavana in the conference room at the Institute.

In the afternoon, they visited Lord Krsna’s birthplace. As they had *darsana* at each altar, the priests garlanded them and put sandalwood paste on their foreheads. They also went down to the prison room where Lord Krsna was born. After having *darsana* of all the Deities, and seeing the wonderfully decorated temple room, they were taken into the VIP lounge. There, two *brahmanas* chanted Sanskrit prayers for about ten minutes, greeting them.

Then the Druze performed *acamana* and were given flowers to offer to the Deities. They were again garlanded, offered *maha-prasadam* and sandalwood paste, gave a donation, then returned to the ISKCON temple.

A press conference with two newspapers and a television station had been scheduled at the temple. Although the first questions were political, Sheikh Hussein deftly sidestepped the politics and the reporters began to ask spiritual questions. Later on in his room, Sheik Hussein told me that while he was speaking to the reporters, the other sheikhs were nudging him and telling him not to reveal the Druze scriptures. Sheik Hussein told them that he wasn’t revealing the Druze scriptures but speaking on the Vedic scriptures. It just happens that the two are the same. The newspaper articles came out this morning.

March 16

I did not go to Govardhana with the party, so this following report was written by Gopakumara Prabhu.

Today we arrived at the Govardhana Palace *asrama.* Kesava Bharati Prabhu received us along with Jayadvaita Maharaja. They invited us into the room with the Deities. The Druze sat down, and the devotees and the sheikhs began a philosophical discussion.

The thing about this meeting is that it wasn’t a formal meeting, and some of the Druze got a bit mental and sleepy. Still, Jayadvaita Swami answered their questions brilliantly, and that woke them to their natural inquisitiveness and appreciation. At least most of them. They asked nice quesitons, such as “What is the significance of Lord Krsna’s pastimes?” and “Can we enter those pastimes?” If God is Brahman, then why do you worship His form? Maharaja described the three aspects of perceiving the sun, which are all true—but one is Brahman, another is Paramatma, and the third is sun planet itself. He described that if you know Krsna the person, then you know all God’s other features.

Regarding entering Krsna’s pastimes, he said that one can enter them by hearing. They asked how one can know authoritatively that Krsna lifted Govardhana Hill. Jayadvaita Maharaja said that this knowledge has been received according to scripture through the disciplic succession.

Then they wanted to walk, so we went down and heard the *bhajana*. Three devotees sang a *bhajana* for the Druze, and by that time they were ready for lunch. They were offered Govardhana *prasadam.*

After lunch we all went to Kusum Sarovara for a short visit, then returned to the *asrama* for another philosophical discussion. This discussion lasted about an hour and a half. They asked about the Sanskrit language, its origin and significance, and about the creative process. The Druze were expecting the version of a “thou shalt be” creation but, from our side, we gave a description of Lord Mahavisnu and the whole step-by-step process of creation. Eventually, Maharaja told them that the Lord glances at the material energy and that the souls then enter into it and start to function as if they had awoken from a long sleep. They were sastisfied with the discussion.

On March 17, the Druze went to Agra to visit the Taj Mahal and other places. In Jaipur, the main activity was touring the palaces of the kings, visit the museum, etc.—a little bit of ordinary tourism. While there, Bibhisana, Theodoros, and I took the opportunity to visit the temples of Radha-Govinda, Radha-Gopinatha, and Radha-Damodara. The hotel owner happened to be a Vaisnava, so offered to take us to the various temples the next morning.

He was ready with his car at 4:30 a.m., and we started on our morning pilgrimage. On the way he picked up a group of elderly women, who were also on a morning pilgrimage tour. We arrived first at Radha-Gopinatha temple, where many ladies were gathered outside the door, chanting *bhajanas* glorifying Radha-Gopinatha. When the doors opened and the *arati* began, the intensity of their chanting increased manyfold.

Then we drove to Radha-Govinda temple. Here there were hundreds of devotees rushing to attend the morning *arati*. When we arrived at the temple area, there were many groups of devotees chanting *bhajanas* in the courtyard. Others were circumambulating the Deities. Scattered around the courtyard were various programs distributing *maha-prasadam.* After offering obeisances, we drove to see Radha-Damodara.

Along the way, the group of ladies traveling with us were constantly chanting *bhajanas*. At the Radha-Damodara temple there was also a large group of devotees attending the *arati*. It was Ekadasi, and we fasted until evening. The owner of the hotel brought us some *prasadam* his wife had cooked in the evening.

The next day we left Jaipur and traveled to Bombay—or as it is now known, Mumbai. Bhima Prabhu sent four cars to the airport to pick us up. When we arrived at Hare Krishna Land, there was an ecstatic welcome *kirtana*. The Druze were offered garlands by his holiness Amiya-vilasa Swami. Then they were shown to their rooms in the guesthouse.

We rested for the balance of the day, because the trip from Jaipur had been long and exhausting. In the evening we all took *prasadam* at the wonderful temple restaurant.

The next morning, the Druze attended Deity greeting. Again, they all offered flowers and incense to their Lordships Sri Sri Gaura-Nitai, Sri Sri Radha-Rasabihari, and Sita-Rama-Laksmana-Hanuman. They then attended the *guru-puja* ceremony, offering flowers and respects to Srila Prabhupada. After the *guru-puja* prayers had been sung, they stayed for some of the Deity *kirtana*. Later, I heard from Gopakumara Prabhu that one of the Druze had some doubt about the fact that they were worshiping Deities. He said that the chanting was okay, the devotees were okay, *everything* was okay, but worshiping Deities? Gopakumara Prabhu answered nicely by saying that this was not idol worship because the forms were not created out of the imagination of some speculative mind but were bona fide forms described in scripture. He also explained that the descriptions were based upon Krsna’s actual form. He pointed out that showing respect to a picture of one’s son is not idol worship. The Druze were satisfied with this answer. In fact, some of the other Druze present were adamant that this understanding was correct.

And I heard from Gopakumara that Tusti-mohana Krsna Prabhu had also explained to them that when the Deity is installed, the spiritual master requests Krsna to enter into the form and accept the devotees’ service. They appreciated that explanation too.

On the same topic of Deity worship, another interesting point came out yesterday during a meeting between Sheikh Hussein and Haridasa Prabhu, who has a television station (more like a television studio) where he records programs. The programs are then played on satellite television in India and around the world. An audience of thirty million watches his program every day. Sheikh Hussein explained to Haridasa Prabhu that the main reason Deity worship is forbidden among the Druze is that they are living in a Muslim country. The Druze have been persecuted by the Muslims, so over time, they gradually relinquished all aspects of Deity worship or worship of form. But he said that upon seeing the Deity worship performed in ISKCON temples, he could see that these were actually the same rituals that are mentioned in his own scriptures. Or at least the essence of Deity worship is present in their scriptures.

This seems to corroborate a theory I have developed over the last while. The theory is that many of the Druze principles, such as the fact that no one can become a Druze, that Deity worship is forbidden, that their scripture is secret, and some other things, are not essential principles of their religion but principles that have developed over time in order to protect themselves from persecution. For example, the idea that no one can become a Druze would certainly pacify the surrounding religions, as there would be no danger of any of their members converting. Obviously, keeping the scripture secret would avoid confrontation and conflict with other religions whose scripture differed. Now Sheikh Hussein has confirmed that the restriction against Deity worship is based on time, place, and circumstance, not on any eternal religious principle. Anyway, that’s my theory right now.

Afterwards, the Druze were taken on a tour of the diorama exhibit at the Mumbai temple. While on the tour, one of the Hinduja brothers arrived. He greeted the Druze enthusiastically and spoke with them for a few minutes. He invited them to come for a walk the next morning on Juhu Beach, and to come to his house where he would serve them coconut water and *maha-prasadam* sweets.

After meeting with Mr. Hinduja, there was an official welcome ceremony in the temple room, and again Sheikh Hussein gave a wonderful speech expressing how the Druze were happy to be present among so many brothers. Suradasa garlanded each of them and presented each with a picture of Radha-Rasabihari.

Sheikh Fuad Aburukun then recited a poem he had composed in glorification of Krsna. Here is the translation of that poem:

In the Name of God, the All-Merciful and Almighty

Besides Lord Krsna I have nobody.

Because You are always my shelter.

If they were to offer me all the treasures of this world,

still no one could win my heart, because it is dedicated to You.

Lord Krsna, please save this land

and give pleasure to the priests and the children.

We are Your devotees and from Your worship we won’t deviate,

even if they break our bones with sticks.

Please forgive Your servants the offenses we commit to You.

Your mercy is bewildering to many people.

You’re the giver of goodness to all this world,

and thus you rightly claim the throne of the Almighty.

We are Yours forever. Our souls are dedicated to You.

We can lay down our lives for the service of Krsna.

Please glance at us with Your sidelong glances

and allow us to enter into Your kingdom.

Signed,

The most unworthy and weak among the devotees,

and a beggar in front of God,

Sheikh Fuad Aburukun

The devotees were in ecstasy to hear this beautiful poem glorifying Krsna.

After the morning program, the Druze went to the restaurant, where they were offered breakfast *prasadam*. Then they went on a small tour of Srila Prabhu-pada’s rooms and other parts of Hare Krsna Land.

In the evening, Haridasa Prabhu met with the whole group of Druze in one of their rooms and discussed the possibility of coming to Israel to make a documentary on the Druze and their relationship with Hare Krsna. He said that he can have this documentary broadcast on the BBC or the Discovery Channel. The Druze were enthusiastic about the idea, and invited him to come. They also offered to assist him in any way needed. The only condition was that he not make a superficial presentation, but rather go deep into the relationship between the two cultures. Haridasa Prabhu was happy about the idea, and is considering going to Israel in a few months to begin preparing the script.

The next morning (March 23), the Druze went out at 6:00 for a morning walk on Juhu Beach. As we passed the Hinduja brothers’ house, the brother we had met the previous day came out and invited us into his house. We sat down in his garden, and he and Sheikh Hussein discussed many things. They actually became good friends, with a wonderful rapport developing between them.

After drinking coconut water and honoring *maha-prasadam* sweets, we headed back to the temple, Mr. Hinduja with us. As we were walking back, he suggested that the Druze should definitely go to Haridwar to bathe in the Ganges. Sheikh Hussein promised that they would go. (We are scheduled to go to Haridwar at the beginning of April.)

Upon arriving at the temple, Mr. Hinduja taught Sheikh Hussein the complete *maha-mantra.* He insisted that he chant the whole mantra from memory. Sheikh Hussein willingly and happily complied, and Fuad Aburukun took out his notebook and wrote all the words down in his notebook. Then Mr. Hinduja suggested that they make a Krsna temple in their Druze villages in Israel. Sheikh Hussein suggested that he could provide land for such a temple, and Mr. Hinduja enthusiastically suggested that we should immediately go ahead with such a project. Then the Druze headed off for a tour of Bombay that Suradasa Prabhu had organized.

That’s it for today. Tonight, a program has been arranged by the Chowpatty temple at a doctor’s house. Parijata-devi dasi has arranged for two appointments with reporters, one tonight at the doctor’s house and the other tomorrow evening at the temple.

Your servant,

Vijnana dasa Vanacari

All glories to Mathura-Vrndavana, our real home. All glories to ISKCON Vaisnavas, who cooperate for pleasing their beloved Srila Prabhupada and spreading Krsna consciousness throughout the world. May this historic Druze pilgrimage inspire us to publish all of Srila Prabhupada’s books in Arabic and to distribute them with gusto. “Srila Narottama dasa Thukura therefore says: s*ri gauda-mandala-bhumi, yeba jane cintamani / ta’ra haya vrajabhume vasa.* Vraja-bhumi refers to Mathura-Vrndavana, and Gauda-mandala-bhumi includes Navadvipa. These two places are nondifferent. Therefore, anyone living in Navadvipa-dhama, knowing Krsna and Sri Caitanya Mahaprabhu to be the same personality, lives in Vrajabhumi, Mathura-Vrndavana. The Lord has made it convenient for the conditioned soul to live in Mathura, Vrndavana, and Navadvipa and thus be directly connected with the Supreme Personality of Godhead. Simply by living in these places, one can immediately come in contact with the Lord. Anyone who executes devotional service in these places certainly goes back home, back to Godhead, after giving up his body.” (*Bhag.* 10.1.28, purport)

“The Lord is one, whether one is Hindu, Muslim or Christian. Therefore, one should chant the holy name of the Lord, regardless of differences in linguistic pronunciation.” (*Bhag.* 7.11.8–12, purport)

“But when we attain transcendental knowledge and are established in the actual, eternal religion of the actual living entity—the spirit soul—then and then only can we attain real, undeniable peace, prosperity, and happiness in the world.” (*Message of Godhead,* 1: “Transcendental Knowledge”

“Regarding the Arabic translation, whether it is good or bad, something should be printed. As you have suggested, let it be printed in India. Our good friend Mr. Brij Ratan Mohatta volunteered to help pay the printing costs of Arabic publication. So in this regard you can send the manuscripts to Gopala Krsna, and he will arrange everything.” (Letter to: Harikesa, May 6, 1977)

March 24, 2001, from Dhira Govinda dasa:

Related to Vijnana Prabhu’s report from March 17–23: When we were living and presenting Krsna consciousness to the Druze in Israel, they confided in us that many of the habits they had adopted, such as meat-eating and coffee-drinking, were taken up in order to conform with Muslim practices. They acknowledged that such things were not part of the original Druze (Muwahidoon) culture. Now, by the association of the devotees and the holy *dhamas,* they are disclosing the same sort of thing regarding Deity worship. I felt great joy when I read the poem about Krsna by Sheikh Fuad Aburukun.

March 29

Today we arrived in Amritsar. Prema-manjari dasi and her husband had arranged a hotel for us. They came to the hotel later and took some of the group to see the Golden Temple of the Sikhs. We spoke with some of the religious leaders there and made an appointment for the next day at 10:00 a.m., when the president of the temple would be there to meet with the Druze.

March 30

Today the Druze met with the President of the Sikh temple for a couple of hours. Afterwards, they toured the Temple and then we left for Chandigarh. We were to stay the night in Chandigarh before going on to Haridwar and Rishikesh the next day.

March 31

I went to Delhi directly, because the permit on one of our vehicles was due to expire by by midnight of the 31st. Tusti-mohana Krsna Prabhu and Bhibhisana took the group to Haridwar and Rishikesh, where they stayed overnight and came the next evening to Delhi.

April 1

Today was spent visiting places in Haridwar and Rishikesh and travelling to Delhi in the evening.

April 2, Rama-navami

The Druze arrived at the temple at about 10:30, and were fortunate to be present for the *agnihotra* ceremony in honor of Lord Rama’s appearance. They were in ecstasy again, and felt really honored to be present at such a function. Sheikh Fuad again came up with one of his spontaneous poems in glorification of Krsna and the devotees. After that, there was an official welcome ceremony in which Gopala Krsna Maharaja and Maha-mantra spoke, followed by Sheikh Hussein. My report is quite sparse because I wasn’t there. If Tusti-mohana Krsna Prabhu could give a more complete description of the events, it may be better.

Your servant,

Vijnana dasa Vanacari

## Villages

**Abu Snan**—Population 7,300. About 50% Druze. Located next to Cfar Yasif in the Acko region.

**Ain-el-Assad**—A Druze village of a few hundred residents. Near Rama’s east side.

**Ain Kunya**—Population about 1,300. About 90% Druze.

**Beit Jann**—Population 6,800. Completely Druze. About ten kilometers west of Tsfat.

**Buq’atha**—Population about 3,000. Completely Druze. Located in Ramat Ha Golan.

**Cfar Yasif**—Population 6,000. Contains only a few hundred Druze. Maqam al-Khidr, a holy site associated with Elijah the Prophet, is in Cfar Yasif. Located in the vicinity of Acko.

**Daliyat-el-Carmel**—Situated about ten kilometers southeast of Haifa on the Carmel mountain range. Population 10,100. About 95% Druze. Major families, Nasr al-Din and Halabi. A few kilometers further to the southeast is the holy site Muqraka, sacred to both Druze and Christians.

**Fassoutta**—Small Christian village in Upper Galil. Population about 2,000.

**Hurfeish**—Population about 3,000. About 90% Druze. Near village is Druze holy site Nabi Sabalan.

**Ilaboon**—Mostly Christian village near Maghar. Population about 2,000.

**Jatt**—Also known as Galil. Population about 1,000. Completely Druze. Located about six kilometers from Abu Snan.

**Jish**—Also known as Gush Halav. Mostly Christian village in Upper Galil. Population about 4,000.

**Julis**—Home of Sheikh Tarif Amin. Situated about ten kilometers east of Acko and about two kilometers southeast of Cfar Yasif. Population about 3,800. Completely Druze.

**Kisra**—Population about 1,800. Completely Druze. Located near Samiah.

**Magd-el-Champs**—Population 6,400. Completely Druze. The northernmost village in the Golan Heights.

**Maghar**—Population 12,800. About 50% Druze. About ten kilometers southeast of Karmiel.

**Masade**—Population about 4,000. Completely Druze. About four kilometers south of Magd-el-Champs. Near the village is the Druze holy site Maqam al-Yafuri.

**Miilia**—Small Christian village near Maalot. Population about 2,000.

**Osafia**—Situated about seven kilometers southeast of Haifa. Population 7,400. About 75% Druze. Contains holy place Maqam Abu Abdallah. Major families, Abu-Rooken and Mansur.

**Peqiin**—Also called Buqaya. Population about 4,000. About 70% Druze. An ancient village with many Jewish holy sites.

**Rama**—Population 5,900. About 25% Druze. About six kilometers east of Karmiel.

**Sajur**—Population about 1,800. Completely Druze. Located next to Rama on the west side.

**Samiah**—Population about 1,500. About 75% Druze. Located between Beit Jann and Maalot. Major family, Falach.

**Shefaram**—About 20% Druze. Population 20,400.

**Yanook**—Population about 2,000. Completely Druze. Contains Druze holy site Nabi Shams. Situated near Maalot.

**Yarka**—Population 8,000. Completely Druze. Major families, Mu’addi and Qadamani. Located near Acko. Israeli Druze population in Israel at end of 1989, about 80,000.

## Glossary

A: Arabic or from Arabic culture

H: Hebrew or from Jewish or Israeli culture

S: Sanskrit or from Vedic culture

**Abed Allah** (A): Servant of God.

**Ahalan** (A): Hello.

**Al Hakim Bi-Amr Allah** (A): An Egyptian king of the early Eleventh Century, considered by the Druze to be a manifestation of the Godhead.

**Allah** (A): The Greatest.

**Allah akbar** (A): God is great.

**Asrama** (S): Spiritual order or class, according to the Vedic system.

**Bhagavad-gita** (S): The Song of God, spoken by Sri Krsna five thousand years ago. *Bhagavad-gita* is the most famous Vedic literature.

**Bhajana** (S): Glorification of God performed by devotees, usually sitting down and with traditional Indian instruments.

**Bhakta** (S): A servant of Krsna.

**Bhakti-yoga** (S): The process of linking with Krsna by serving Him with love and devotion.

**Bharata-varsa** (S): A name for India, especially referring to India during the Vedic Age.

**Bhoga** (S): foodstuffs that are meant to be prepared for and offered to the Lord.

**Brahma-muhurta** (S): A period before sunrise when it is especially auspicious to perform spiritual activities.

**Brahmacari** (S): An unmarried celibate male student in Vedic culture.

**Brahmacarini** (S): The term used in ISKCON for a woman who is not married.

**Brahmana** (S): The intellectual class in the Vedic social order.

**Brahmavadi** (S): A type of impersonalist.

**Cain** (H): Yes.

**Cfar** (H): Village.

**Chadar** (S): A cloth, usually worn around the shoulders.

**Chapatti** (S): A type of round Indian bread.

**Choli** (S): Short shirt worn under *sari* in Vedic culture.

**Dandavats** (S): Prostrated obeisances.

**Davener** (H): One who prays according to the Jewish tradition. To “daven”means to sway back and forth while praying.

**Dhama** (S): Holy place.

**Dharma** (S): One’s occupational duty.

**Dhoti** (S): The cloth wrapped around the bottom part of a man’s body in Vedic culture.

**Dwarak-diwali** (A): A cooked preparation of grape leaves rolled around rice.

**Gayatri** (S): A prayer recited three times every day by devotees who have taken Brahminical initiation.

**Gematria** (H): The system of ascribing numerical equivalents to Hebrew letters for the purpose of scriptural understanding.

**Ghee** (S): Clarified butter.

**Gurukula** (S): The spiritual master’s school. A school where children are trained in spiritual life.

**Guru-puja** (S): Worship of the spiritual master.

**Hadar** (H): The middle section of Haifa.

**Hari-nama** (S): Chanting of God’s holy names. Public singing of God’s names.

**Hatha-yoga** (S): A physical yoga process meant for controlling the senses and mind.

**ISKCON**: Acronym for the International Society for Krsna Consciousness.

**Istha-gosthi** (S): A forum for Vaisnava discussion.

**Japa mala** (S): Beads for chanting the names of God.

**Kabbalist** (H): One who studies and practices the mystical Jewish texts.

**Kali-yuga** (S): The current age in Vedic cosmology, characterized by degradation.

**Karatalas** (S): Traditional Indian hand-cymbals.

**Karmi** (S): One whose actions create reactions within material nature.

**Kepah** (H): Yarmulkah. Small head covering worn by religious Jews.

**Kirtana** (S): Glorification of the Lord, usually performed by singing His holy names.

**Krsna** (S): According to the *Vedas,* the Supreme Personality of Godhead and source of all Divine incarnations.

**Ksatriya** (S): The administrative and warrior class in the Vedic social order.

**Kufr** (A): Heathen.

**Kurta** (S): An Indian style shirt, often worn by members of ISKCON.

**Kuruksetra** (S): The place in India where a famous battle was fought about 5,000 years ago. The site where Lord Krsna spoke *Bhagavad-gita* to Arjuna.

**Labneh** (A): A popular Arabic cheese, often sour.

**Lahmed Vahvnik** (H): One of thirty-six great souls who appear in this world to bring people closer to God.

**Maha** (S or A): Great, or large (S). The eyes of a deer (A).

**Maha-mantra** (S): The most effective sound vibration for developing spiritual consciousness. The *maha-mantra* is: Hare Krsna, Hare Krsna, Krsna Krsna, Hare Hare, Hare Rama, Hare Rama, Rama Rama, Hare Hare.

**Mangala-arati** (S): The early morning ceremony in Vedic temples, performed during the most auspicious part of the day.

**Mantra** (S): Sound vibration for delivering the mind from material existence.

**Masah** (H): Long, difficult journey.

**Masbachah** (A): A string of beads for chanting God’s names.

**Matajis** (S): Mothers.

**Maya** (S): Illusion.

**Mayavadi** (S): An impersonalist who considers the form of God to be illusion, or *maya.*

**Melooeem** (H): Military reserve.

**Mifaked** (H): Commander. Mifakedet is used for female commanders.

**Mitzbe** (H): A small, pioneering communal settlement in Israel.

**Mitzvot** (H): Good deeds, according to the commandments of the Bible and other Jewish scriptures.

**Moatsah-makomit** (H): Village council. Building where village council congregates.

**Moazin** (A): One who sings the prayer from the mosque to the Muslim community.

**Moshav** (H): A type of semi-communal Jewish Israeli settlement.

**Mount Hermon** (H): Situated on the Syrian-Israeli border at the top of the Golan, Mount Hermon’s peak is the tallest in the region.

**Mount Tabor** (H): A mountain in Israel about eight kilometers from Nazareth.The site of the Transfiguration of Jesus.

**Moshav** (H): A type of semi-communal Israeli settlement.

**Muwahhidoon** (A): The one, or eternal, religion.

**Nama-hatta** (S): The marketplace of the holy name. A congregation of people meant to glorify Krsna.

**Oleh-hadash** (H): A Jew who has recently become a citizen of Israel.

**Panca-tattva** (S): Five aspects of the Absolute Truth, represented by Lord Caitanya, Lord Nityananda, Sri Advaita Acarya, Sri Gadadhara and Sri Srivasa Thukura.

**Parampara** (S): The chain of disciplic succession by which we receive spiritual knowledge.

**Peos** (H): The long sideburns worn by many religious Jews.

**Pita** (H and A): A popular Mid-Eastern bread.

**Ploogah** (H): Platoon.

**Prabhu** (S): Master.

**Prasadam** (S): The mercy of the Lord in the form of the remnants of His foodstuffs. Spiritual food.

**Punya** (S): Pious activity.

**Ramabasis:** Residents of Rama.

**Ramadan** (A): A holy month for Muslims when they fast during the day.

**Ramat Ha Golan** (H): A tract of land in northeastern Israel, taken from Syria during the 1967 war. Today, Ramat Ha Golan contains four Druze and one Aloine village, as well as some Jewish settlements.

**Rasa** (S): One’s specific type of relationship with the Lord.

**Rehov** (H): Street.

**Rehov Dizengoff** (H): Tel Aviv’s main shopping and recreation street.

**Sabji** (S): Cooked vegetable preparation.

**Sadhana** (S): The regulative practices of spiritual life.

**Sadhu-sanga** (S): Association with saintly persons.

**Saktya-avesa avatara** (S): One who is specially empowered with a specific potency of the Lord.

**Samadhi** (S): A blissful state of equanimity and peacefulness.

**Samne** (A): Clarified butter. Ghee.

**Samosas** (S): A preparation of rolled dough filled with cooked vegetables.

**Sanatana dharma** (S): Devotional service to God, the eternal occupation of the living entity.

**Sankirtana** (S): Complete glorification of God.

**Sastra** (S): Divinely revealed scripture.

**Shabbat** (H): Saturday, the seventh day of the week.

**Sharwahl** (A): Pants with baggy mid-section worn by religious Druze.

**Sheikh** (A): A holy man who has entered into the religious order.

**Shomrone** (H): A name for the West Bank occupied territories.

**Shukran** (A): Thank you.

**Shuuk** (H and A): Market.

**Sikha** (S): The tuft of hair that a male devotee of Krsna wears on the back of his head.

**Srimad-Bhagavatam** (S): The explanation of the essence of Vedic wisdom. Composed by Srila Vyasadeva five-thousand years ago, the *Srimad-Bhagavatam* directly describes the pastimes of the Supreme Personality of Godhead.

**Supersoul** (S): The Paramatma, or incarnation of the Lord within the heart of each living entity. The Supersoul is situated next to the soul within each body.

**Tabaro** (A): Donation.

**Taquiyya** (A): The Druze socio-political philosophy whereby they externally conform to the practices of the predominant culture, but internally remain true to the Muwahhidoon.

**Tilaka** (S): Clay from sacred river used to auspiciously mark the body. The most prominent tilak mark is on the forehead.

**Tsahal** (H): Israeli army.

**Ulpan** (H): An intensive course in Hebrew.

**Vadi nis nas** (A): Vadi means “valley.” The Vadi nis nas is an Arabic section in Haifa’s Hadar district.

**Vaisnava** (S): One who worships Visnu as the Supreme Lord.

**Vanaprastha** (S): The spiritual order for those retired from family life.

**Varna-sankara** (S): Unwanted progeny.

**Veda** (S): Knowledge.

**Yad-lachim** (H): An Israeli anti-cult organization.

**Yeda** (H): Knowledge.

**Yeshiva** (H): A school for the study of Jewish scripture and Jewish law.
